# Roadmap

**Status:** Proposed
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Deciders:** Alimardon (order and vetoes), Claude (execution and defaults)

The plan from phase 0 (a base that boots, updates and rolls back in CI) to a first public release and beyond. It follows the ranked [design principles](DESIGN-PRINCIPLES.md) and the decisions in ADR-002 to ADR-008. The guiding idea is **fewest parts**: one tool (`edel`, inside the image and in CI), one file format (TOML with a format number), one image pipeline (`edel image build`, also for the live USB, add-ons and fleet images), one update path (a whole slot, carrying the bootloader; add-ons ride the same path), one file that describes a machine (`settings.toml`, which also holds the shell, output and update settings the compositor and shell-ui watch). Every milestone says which parts exist after it and which were deliberately not added.

## How this roadmap is used

- Work starts when Alimardon says go. Claude then works through it step by step while Alimardon is away: one branch and one PR per step, merged when CI is green, then the box is ticked here and the next step starts.
- Each step is a few hundred lines and ends with **Done when:** a check CI runs (cargo tests, a QEMU boot with a line on the serial console, a screenshot pixel check, a container run). Claude has no hardware; Alimardon's trials on their laptops are the only hardware feedback and arrive as comments and `edel report` files.
- When a step turns out wrong or too big, Claude changes the plan in place (splits, reorders, rewrites "Done when") and says so under "Changes to the plan" at the end, dated.
- Open questions are not waited on. Claude takes the default listed under "Decisions taken by default" and lists it there; Alimardon overrules by commenting in the project. They can reorder or veto anything.
- Things that always wait for Alimardon's own words: anything outside this repository (new repositories, accounts, services, repository settings such as branch protection), spending money (runners, storage, domains, devices), publishing to the public (a release, a pre-release, a website, a registry), deleting data, and changing the license.
- Package names, crate names and versions marked "check in CI" are from memory; the first CI run of that step verifies them and the step's notes are corrected afterwards.

## Where we are

Phase 0 is merged to main (2026-10-01):

- `edel image build DEF.toml` (Rust, `crates/edel`) builds a container image (tar.gz) and a VM disk from Alpine 3.24 packages with apk, overlay files and OpenRC services. Definitions are `images/container.toml` and `images/vm.toml`, format 1.
- The VM disk is GPT: an EFI system partition with GRUB, slot A and slot B. GRUB counts boot tries per slot in `grubenv` with RAUC's variable names and falls back to the other slot after three failed tries. The `edel-boot-ok` service runs `edel-update mark-good` once the system is up.
- `edel-update` (a busybox sh script in `images/files/ab`) has `status`, `install IMAGE` (writes the other slot, checks sha256, fresh UUID, flips the boot order) and `mark-good`.
- CI builds everything inside a privileged `alpine:3.24` container, runs the container image, boots the VM with QEMU and OVMF to the login prompt, and runs the A/B test (install an update, reboot into it, install a broken update, watch it fall back). It reports image sizes.
- Decided: license GPL-3.0-or-later; our own updater (`edel update`), RAUC is not packaged; the name is Edel OS.

Since then (2026-10-02), M1 replaced `edel-update` with `edel update` in Rust and added the data partition, the read-only root with `/etc`, `/home`, `/var` and `/root` on it, the watchdog guard, signed updates from a URL and the boot loader riding along; M2 added the settings file, `edel install` and the A/B test on a freshly installed disk; M3 added versions, budgets, the laptop image, 4 GiB slots, `edel report` and the release scripts (publishing waits for the release keys); M4.0 made images out of feature files and M4.1 added the desktop image and the CI display harness. Known gap, kept on purpose: GRUB writes the try counter at every boot.

| Milestone | Phase | In one line |
|---|---|---|
| M1 One update path, finished | 1, now | Data survives, hangs fall back, rollback by hand, signed updates from a URL, GRUB rides along |
| M2 One file describes and installs a machine | 1, now | Settings file v1, `edel install`, fresh installs tested like updates |
| M3 Release train, kept small | 1, now | Versions, budgets, a laptop image, a preview kept forever, upgrade from every kept release |
| M4 The compositor | next | Floating and tiling with title bars, in QEMU and on a laptop |
| M5 shell-ui, presets, settings, tokens | next | Classic, Mac-like, Windows-like, Hive with one button; appearance and shortcuts; the first adaptive app |
| M6 Apps, the basics, the installer page | next | Flatpak, a default app set, portals, store, printing, input methods, accessibility, behaviour settings, install from Settings |
| M7 Extend: developer mode, add-ons, fleets, servers, profiles | next | Everything Versatile and Scalable, as files and signed add-on images |
| M8 First public release | next | Encryption, monthly rebuilds, Alpine branch bump, the previous release kept alive, release gates, platform level 1 |
| M9 Phones, tablets, arm64 | later | arm64 in CI, form factors, touch, a phone image |
| M10 The compatibility promise | later | App platform on the oldest kept release, runtime archive, driver rebuilds |
| M11 Enterprise appliances and the long channel | later | A verified root, TPM unlock, audit, hardening checks in CI; the ten-year channel and a systemd adapter add-on once someone can carry them (ADR-009) |
| M12 Glasses and pocket PCs | an idea | Display glasses as the screen of a pocket-sized PC, the desktop fixed in space; written down on 2026-10-07, not scheduled |
| M13 Devices as one, the deep parts | later, after the stable OS | Spikes for apps that travel between devices and devices working as one computer (ADR-011) |

Every milestone ends with the ab-test, boot-test and install-test still green; a step that breaks them is not done.

**Next, in order (2026-10-06):** the fix-first round that two outside reviews led to (ADR-009): M3.6 (a desktop image to try on a laptop), M5.25, M5.27 and M5.26 (one name in Settings, the command line and the file, one owner for every fact, and tab completion), M5.19 to M5.24 (the desktop's blockers), M1.10 to M1.13 and M3.7 to M3.9 (safe to publish); then M5.6 and the rest of M5. Names in this roadmap are today's until M5.25 renames them (ADR-008's same names decision). The first preview waits for M3.7, M3.8, one narrow review of the updater, the guard and the signing path, and Alimardon's release key (M3.4).

## M1 (phase 1, now): One update path, finished

**Goal:** a VM installs a signed, gzip-compressed update straight from a URL, keeps `/home`, `/var` and its own `/etc` changes across the update, comes back on the old slot when the new one hangs (not only when it crashes), can be rolled back by hand, and the bootloader rides along in the same update. Alimardon can run `edel update install https://.../release.toml` on a VM and watch `edel update status`.

**Why first:** Reliable is principle 1, and every later feature is installed, copied and rolled back through this path. Today an update would erase the user's data.

**Depends on:** nothing. **Steps, in the order Claude does them next:**

- [x] **1. `edel update` in Rust replaces the shell script; rollback by hand.** `crates/edel/src/update.rs` (status, install IMAGE, mark-good, rollback) and `grubenv.rs` (parse and write the 1024-byte block, shared with `boot.rs`); slot discovery from `/proc/self/mountinfo` and `/sys/dev/block`; sha256 via the `sha2` crate; e2fsck, tune2fs and blockdev via `Command`. `rollback` flips ORDER to the other slot and refuses when that slot is off. `edel image build` copies `target/release/edel` into the rootfs (it is already built inside `alpine:3.24` against the image's musl); `images/files/ab/usr/sbin/edel-update` is deleted; `edel-boot-ok` and the ab-test files call `edel update`.
  Done when: cargo tests cover the grubenv round trip, ORDER/OK/TRY transitions for install, mark-good and rollback, and refusal to write the running slot or roll back to an off slot, against a mocked sysfs tree in a tempdir; ab-test passes with the Rust updater and gains a step that rolls back by command and boots the old slot. Notes: keep RAUC's variable names; pure functions take the env text and partition numbers so they test without a disk. `ci/build.sh` sets `CARGO_HOME=/src/.cargo-home` and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/src/target` so `actions/cache` keyed on `Cargo.lock` has something to cache. No apk packaging yet: `packages/` and `ci/packages.sh` arrive with the first step that needs apk resolution (a patched Alpine package, or M7). A lock (a file under `/run/edel` holding the pid, replaced when that process is gone) refuses a second `edel update` while one runs; the ESP is mounted only while grubenv is written, never in fstab, as the script does.
- [x] **2. Partition 4, `edel-data`.** `boot.rs` Layout gains partition 4 (ext4, label `edel-data`, 64 MiB in the image) after slot B so the slots stay partitions 2 and 3; `[vm] data_mib`; OpenRC sysinit service `edel-data` (after devfs, before the boot runlevel) runs `edel boot mount-data`: on first boot grows partition 4 to the end of the disk (`growpart DISK 4` from `cloud-utils-growpart`, which is in the v3.24 index (checked 2026-10-02), then `resize2fs`; it handles the relocated GPT backup header and the in-use disk in one call), mounts `LABEL=edel-data` at `/data`, prints `edel-data: mounted /data`.
  Done when: cargo tests for Layout (four partitions, no overlap, disk size, every GPT name 24 characters or fewer: longer names broke fastboot on the OnePlus 6); boot-test greps `edel-data: mounted /data`; a boot-test variant on a copy of the image grown by 3 GiB (`truncate`) shows `df /data` above 2 GiB (ext4 keeps about 7 percent for itself, measured in CI). Notes: container image untouched; sysinit because bootmisc and syslog write to `/var` early. If growpart is unavailable: `sfdisk --relocate gpt-bak-std`, `echo ', +' | sfdisk --no-reread -N 4`, then `partx -u`.
- [x] **3. Persistent `/home` and `/var`.** `edel boot mount-data` creates `/data/home` and `/data/var` and bind-mounts them over `/home` and `/var`. On every boot, before the bind, it copies into `/data/var` any top-level entry (and any `/var/lib` and `/var/log` entry) that exists in the slot's `/var` but not on `/data`, keeping symlinks such as `/var/run -> /run`, so an update whose packages add directories under `/var` boots with them present. `mark-good` records a fallback it finds (a slot it switches off) in `/data/edel/last-fallback.toml` (from, to, date) for shell-ui to show in M5.
  Done when: ab-test step 1 writes `/home/ab-marker` and `/var/ab-marker`; step 2, running on slot B, asserts both exist and that `/var/lib/ci-new` (carried by the update image in `ci/ab-test/files/var/lib/ci-new/`) exists; step 3, after the fallback, asserts the markers again and that `last-fallback.toml` names the broken slot. Notes: Flatpak and containers land in `/var` later and inherit survival for free.
- [x] **4. Read-only root with `/etc` changes on `/data`.** fstab mounts `/` with `ro`; `edel boot mount-data` overlay-mounts `/etc` (lower = the slot's `/etc`, upper = `/data/etc/upper`, work = `/data/etc/work`); `/tmp` on tmpfs (Alpine does not do this by default); `overlay` added to the kernel `modules=`. Before OpenRC starts services, `edel boot mount-data` adds the system users and groups the slot has but the upper `passwd`, `group` and `shadow` lack, since a machine's own copies would hide users a later slot adds (Duranium repairs this every boot); hostname, machine-id and password backups never come from the slot.
  Done when: boot-test greps `/ ... ro` from `/proc/mounts` printed on serial and `touch /usr/x` fails with EROFS; ab-test step 2 asserts a hostname changed in step 1 and the ssh host key fingerprint survived the update, and that a system user only the update image adds (in `ci/ab-test/files`) is in `getent passwd` after step 1 created a normal user. Notes: the real work is hunting stray writes to `/` service by service. OpenRC reads `/etc/rc.conf` early; if the sysinit ordering proves fragile, the fallback is our own init script passed with `mkinitfs -i FILE` (a copy of Alpine's initramfs-init with the `/data`, `/etc` overlay and `/var` mounts before switch_root), which is its own PR with a cargo-tested generator and a boot-test, since mkinitfs has no hook mechanism. The upper directory is the list of what this machine changed, and `edel settings export` lists it (AerynOS lesson 1).
- [x] **5. A hanging slot falls back too.** `edel boot guard` replaces the mark-good call in `edel-boot-ok`: an OpenRC service started first in sysinit (after devfs, before mdev and hwdrivers) with `command_background=yes` and a pidfile, so start() returns at once and the guard runs on in the background (a service that blocked until the default runlevel would wait for itself). It opens `/dev/watchdog`, pets it every second until every health file named by `EDEL_HEALTH` in `/usr/lib/os-release` exists (`default-runlevel` means `/run/edel/default-reached`, which `edel-boot-ok`, kept in the default runlevel with `depend() { after *; }`, touches; `compositor` later means `/run/edel/session/ready`) or `EDEL_HEALTH_TIMEOUT` (120 s) passes; healthy means mark-good, magic close and exit, so no daemon stays; timeout means stop petting, the hardware resets, GRUB counts the try. `/run/openrc/softlevel` is never consulted: OpenRC writes it at the start of a runlevel change, while services may still hang. `edel image build` writes the `EDEL_*` keys into os-release from `[image] health` and `health_timeout` in the definition (one standard file, no new format; the version joins it in M3). Kernel `modules=` adds `i6300esb` and `softdog`; `ci/vm.sh` adds `-device i6300esb -action watchdog=reset`; `panic=10` on the cmdline covers plain crashes.
  Done when: cargo tests show the health check accepts the sentinel files only, not softlevel; ab-test gains two cases: (hang) an update whose default runlevel has a service that blocks forever, so the sentinel never appears; (freeze) an update whose default runlevel disables panic reboot and runs `echo c > /proc/sysrq-trigger`. Both come back on the previous slot with the broken one at ok=0. Notes: the ab-test image sets `health_timeout = 30` (the i6300esb default period) so the six failed tries cost minutes, not a quarter hour; release images keep 120. The freeze case is what tests the hardware watchdog; softdog alone would catch the hang. Older QEMU spells it `-watchdog i6300esb -watchdog-action reset` (check in CI); check that linux-virt ships `i6300esb` as a module; laptops have iTCO, softdog is the fallback. Start the guard in sysinit when `/dev/watchdog` already exists there (the modules load from the initramfs), so a hang in sysinit or boot is caught too (check in CI). A kernel panic reboots through `panic=10`, and Alpine's initramfs panics instead of waiting at its recovery shell when `panic=` is set (checked 2026-10-02).
- [x] **6. Signed releases, two keys, no downgrades.** `release.toml` (format = 1: version, channel, date, `images[]` with name, file, sha256, size, min_size, loader_version) plus `release.toml.sig`; `edel release sign|verify` with ed25519 (`ed25519-dalek`; check the musl build in CI); two public keys baked at `/usr/share/edel/keys/` through `[release] public_keys` in the image definition; PR runs use a throwaway key; `edel update install` refuses a manifest that fails verification, an image whose sha256 differs, and a version not newer than the running slot unless `--allow-downgrade`. The manifest is the one file an old slot reads from the future, so its reader ignores fields it does not know (the signature still covers them) and refuses only a format it does not know; `docs/FORMATS.md` states the stepping-stone rule: a manifest format bump publishes the new format under a new file name and keeps `release.toml` pointing at a release whose `edel` reads both (ADR-008).
  Done when: cargo tests (sign then verify, tampered manifest, wrong key, downgrade, a signed manifest with an unknown extra field verifies and installs, one with `format = 2` is refused naming the format); ab-test refuses a manifest with one flipped byte (`refused: signature` on serial) and then installs the good one. Notes: one key scheme for images, add-ons and manifests. A machine two formats behind installs the stepping stone, then the newer release at its next check, with no extra command (AerynOS users rerun theirs by hand). The second key exists from the first install so a rotation never strands a machine; the real key moves to a GitHub secret in M3 and an offline backup is Alimardon's job.
- [x] **7. Update from a URL, compressed, any slot size.** `edel update install URL` and `edel update check` (`ureq` with rustls, `flate2` for gzip), streaming the `.ext4.gz` into the slot while hashing; `edel image build` shrinks the slot image with `resize2fs -M` before gzip and records `min_size`, and emits `.ext4.gz` and `.img.gz` next to the raw files; install refuses an image whose `min_size` exceeds the slot and runs `resize2fs` on the target partition after the write, so a small image fills any slot; `ci/ab-test.sh` serves `out/` with `python3 -m http.server` on the runner and the VM reaches it at 10.0.2.2.
  Done when: ab-test step 1 installs from `http://10.0.2.2:8000/release.toml` with no second disk, using an update built with a smaller `slot_mib` than the running disk, and the VM boots it with `df /` showing the full slot; sizes.sh shows compressed sizes. Notes: rustls on musl, prefer the ring provider (check in CI); `file://` paths stay for USB installs; no delta updates until a measurement says the base is too big.
- [x] **8. The bootloader rides along in the update.** `edel image build` places the GRUB EFI binary, `grub.cfg` and a `loader.toml` (version = hash of both) under `/usr/lib/edel/boot/` in the rootfs; `edel update mark-good`, only after the slot is confirmed, compares with the ESP's `loader.toml` and installs the new loader by write to `.new`, fsync, rename, keeping `BOOTX64.EFI.prev`. `grub.cfg` stays compatible with both slots.
  Done when: ab-test builds the update image with `--loader-tag ci` so its loader differs; after the slot boots and is confirmed the ESP's `loader.toml` shows the new version; cargo test for the rename sequence in a tempdir. Notes: the loader is the one piece outside the A/B rollback: firmware loads one `BOOTX64.EFI`, and if it does not start, no slot is reachable and `.prev` only helps a person with a USB stick. The protection is that every loader transition is exercised in CI (M3.5 boots the old preview's ESP through the swap and once more) and that loader changes stay rare and reviewed; swap after confirmation, never at install time; the env block is never replaced; UEFI only. Call `syncfs` after the write and after the rename: a rename on FAT is not guaranteed atomic (AerynOS's blsforme works around vfat corruption).
- [x] **9. Flatpak on the musl base, checked early (standalone spike).** A CI-only definition `ci/flatpak/vm.toml` (vm plus flatpak, bubblewrap, dbus); `ci/flatpak-test.sh` adds Flathub as user `ci` (`flatpak --user`, the default for every later app install, so the data lives in `/home` and no polkit is needed), installs `org.freedesktop.Platform` (current branch, check), runs `flatpak run --command=sh org.freedesktop.Platform -c 'echo hello from glibc'`, and installs the oldest runtime Flathub still serves; `docs/SPIKE-flatpak.md` records the verdict, download sizes, whether unprivileged user namespaces work with Alpine's kernel config, and what `apk info -R flatpak` says about polkit arriving anyway.
  Done when: serial shows `hello from glibc` for the newest runtime, and the oldest runtime's result is recorded in `docs/SPIKE-flatpak.md`. Notes: no GUI and no compositor needed, so it runs now and retires the ADR-003 and ADR-005 risk for a few CI minutes.

- [x] **10. No watchdog, still no stuck slot.** When `edel boot guard` finds no watchdog at all (a cloud VM that offers none, a kernel without softdog such as linux-virt), it restarts the machine itself once `EDEL_HEALTH_TIMEOUT` passes, after `sync`, so GRUB counts the try exactly as a watchdog reset would; with a watchdog it behaves as today. Found by the outside review of 2026-10-06 (R1): today the guard prints that the watchdog will restart the machine and exits, and with no watchdog nothing does.
  Done when: cargo tests for the choice (a watchdog, softdog only, none); an ab-test case boots an update whose default runlevel hangs, with QEMU's watchdog device left out, and the VM comes back on the previous slot with the broken one at ok=0. Notes: a reboot from user space can itself wait on a hung service, so the guard forces it (`reboot -f`, check in CI). Done with: the guard calls `sync` and the `reboot` system call itself, which is what `reboot -f` does, with no program to start; ab-test's hang step (step 4 to 5) now runs slot B without its watchdog drivers, as the VM's linux-virt has no softdog, and the freeze step still proves the hardware watchdog.
- [x] **11. Images name their own health checks.** A feature's `health` may name any check, not only `default-runlevel` and `compositor`: a check is a sentinel `/run/edel/health/NAME` that the feature's own service writes, such as a server's `network` once its default route answers or an edge device's job once it runs, and `edel image check` refuses a health name that no feature in the image writes. The guard waits for all of them as today, so an update that breaks a server's network falls back instead of being confirmed (R5 of the 2026-10-06 review, ADR-009).
  Done when: cargo tests for the names and the refusal; an ab-test case whose update breaks the network names `network`, the check never passes, and the VM comes back on the previous slot. Notes: the two names of today keep their files (`/run/edel/default-reached`, `/run/edel/session/ready`); a check must finish well inside `health_timeout`. Done with: a feature's file writes `$health_dir/NAME`, which `edel image check` looks for in the image's features' files; the guard's timeout line names the checks not reached; no image lists a check of its own yet, so ab-test's network case adds `network` to slot B's os-release with a service of its own, and takes the networking service out.
- [x] **12. Slots stay byte-identical after install.** GRUB and fstab find a slot by its partition (GPT partition label or PARTUUID), never by its filesystem UUID; `edel install` and `edel update` write a slot image unchanged, with no new filesystem UUID and no resize2fs (a read-only root needs no free space), so a slot's bytes stay those that were signed for its whole life and a verified root (M11.1) can check them at every boot (ADR-009). A disk-layout change, recorded in `docs/FORMATS.md`.
  Done when: install-test and ab-test pass, and a new check finds that slot A of the installed disk, and slot B after the update, hash to exactly the image that was written. Notes: two slots holding the same image share a filesystem UUID, so nothing may find a slot by it (`/dev/disk/by-uuid` included, check in CI); the new `grub.cfg` reaches machines installed earlier with the loader (M1.8), and M3.5 proves it once a preview exists. Done with: GRUB's `probe --part-uuid` and `root=PARTUUID=`; Alpine's initramfs finds that partition but busybox `mount` cannot take `PARTUUID=` (the first run panicked at "Mounting root: failed"), so the image builder hands `mkinitfs -i` Alpine's own init with one change, generated at each build and refused when Alpine's init no longer has its place, that reads the device from the kernel's partition events; `edel update` checks a slot with `e2fsck -fn`, which writes nothing; a slot's file system has no journal (`mkfs.ext4 -O ^has_journal`), since Linux writes a journal's superblock at every mount, read-only ones too, and the second run found a booted slot B 6 bytes off its image there; an image's slot A is its update image itself; install-test wants the installer's `slot A is the running slot byte for byte`, and ab-test hashes slot B after it booted against `release.toml` and finds both slots their shrunk size. Nothing in the images reads `/dev/disk/by-uuid` (the data partition is partition 4, the ESP its label).
- [x] **13. The clock is right on every machine.** The `machine` feature sets the clock from the network with busybox `ntpd` (no new package) once the network is up, and again daily where crond runs; a settings file key, named in the step (ADR-008), gives other servers, such as a cloud provider's own, absent meaning `pool.ntp.org`. A laptop whose clock battery died otherwise fails every certificate check, so updates over `https://` fail (R4 of the 2026-10-06 review).
  Done when: boot-test starts a VM whose clock QEMU sets years back (`-rtc base=2020-01-01`) and serial shows the right year once the network is up (the runner's network, check in CI). Notes: no time daemon stays running on desktops; servers may keep `ntpd` running, which their uptime serves. Done with: the key is `region.time_servers`, one to four host names on the Region page beside the time zone; the `machine` feature's `edel-clock` service starts `/usr/libexec/edel-clock` in the background after the network, which runs `ntpd -n -q` with up to 20 s, up to three times 5 s apart (since 2026-10-08, after a CI runner's first try went unanswered), prints one line and writes the hardware clock, so no image keeps a time daemon and nothing waits for it; no feature runs crond, so there is no daily re-sync yet (each start sets the clock); boot-test's third boot starts the VM in 2020 and wants 2026 or later.

**Parts after M1:** Linux kernel, Alpine base (busybox, OpenRC, apk), GRUB, `edel`. **Deliberately not added:** RAUC, ostree, systemd, a watchdog daemon, a separate update agent, an apk repository of our own, delta or zstd updates, LUKS, an initramfs change.

**Principles check:** Reliable drove the data partition, the guard, signing, rollback by hand and the loader swap after confirmation; Simple kept one tool, one partition layout and one standard file for image facts; Efficient won gzip and the shrunken image but lost the per-boot grubenv write to Reliable.

## M2 (phase 1, now): One file describes and installs a machine

**Goal:** `edel settings export` on one VM and `edel settings apply` on another give the same users, hostname and ssh keys; a blank disk becomes an Edel machine from a settings file; CI tests a fresh install, then an update and a rollback on the installed disk, as principle 1 requires. All on a VM; laptop installs start when `images/laptop.toml` exists (M3).

**Why now:** the settings file is the one way to describe a machine for the installer, the settings app, the shell, servers and fleets; building it before the shell means every later shell feature is a field in an existing file.

**Depends on:** M1.

- [x] **1. Settings file format v1.** `crates/edel/src/settings.rs`: `format = 1`, `[system]` channel/version/variant/developer/profiles, `[users.NAME]` admin/ssh_keys/shell, `[network]` hostname, `[region]` language/keyboard/timezone, `[layout]` preset/tiling/title_bars/device_type, `[displays.NAME]` position/scale/mode/enabled/transform, `[appearance]` wallpaper/color_scheme/accent/font/font_size/cursor_size/icon_size/motion, `[shortcuts]` action = keys, `[default_apps]` browser/files/editor/terminal/mail, `[startup]` apps, `[power]` lid/idle/power_button/on_battery, `[services]` name = true or false, `[updates]` auto/window, `[apps]` flatpak, `[addons]` add (the three customization levels of ADR-007: everything here is level 1); serde without `deny_unknown_fields`, in a library target `edel::settings` inside `crates/edel` whose network and signing dependencies sit behind the binary's default `cli` feature, so the compositor (M4.5), shell-ui (M5.1) and Settings (M5.6) parse with the same code and the same defaults table; unknown keys are collected with `serde_ignored` (check in CI) and enum-valued keys are parsed leniently (an unknown value or a wrong type falls back to the default and is reported, through a `#[serde(other)]` variant or a string mapped after parsing); `edel settings check FILE` refuses an unknown key, value or format, naming the key and listing the allowed values, while the boot reader applies the rest and reports it; a cargo test fails when the structs lack a key listed in the committed, append-only `crates/edel/tests/keys.txt`, and CI fails a PR that deletes a line of it without bumping the format (ADR-008); `docs/settings.md`. Keys that later milestones fill in are parsed now; check and set refuse them with "not supported yet", while the boot apply skips them and reports them, so adding them is not a format bump. Every loader `edel` owns checks `format`; checkers refuse an unknown format with a clear message, while the boot reader that meets a newer `settings.toml` reads `settings.toml.v<N>` beside it for its own N (left by `edel migrate` at a bump) or applies nothing and says so; a key is never removed or renamed within a format (a dropped key stays parsed and is reported as no longer used; an unavoidable rename reads both names); a value naming a preset, profile, add-on or feature this release lacks falls back to the default and is reported, never failing boot; `docs/FORMATS.md` states these rules. ADR-006's example is renamed from `[extensions]` to `[addons]` in the same PR.
  Done when: cargo tests parse the ADR-006 example; `edel settings check` rejects `appearance.acent` naming the key, rejects `appearance.mode = "sepia"` listing light, dark and auto, and rejects format 2, while the boot read of the same file applies every other key, uses the default scheme and reports both; a `settings.toml.v1` beside a format-2 file is used, and with none nothing is applied; a key removed from the structs fails against `keys.txt`; everything round-trips through `toml::to_string`. Notes: `edel migrate` arrives with the first real format bump, when there is a real old file to test on (AerynOS lesson 2); a registry before that is machinery no principle asks for.
- [x] **2. `edel settings apply`, at boot and on demand.** Applies hostname, users (busybox adduser; admin means the `admin` group; users without a password get `*` in `/etc/shadow`, never adduser's `!`, which OpenSSH treats as a locked account), `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` and the developer flag; OpenRC boot service `edel-settings` with `depend() { before hostname; need localmount; }` applies the offline sections of `/data/edel/settings.toml`, seeded on first boot in this order: a volume labelled `EDEL-SEED` holding `settings.toml`, `/EFI/edel/settings.toml` on the ESP, `/usr/share/edel/settings.toml`; idempotent. Sections that need the network (`[apps]`) are applied only when a user or Settings calls apply, in the background.
  Done when: `ci/system-test.sh` builds a VM with a seed file creating user `ci` with a CI-generated ssh key, boots with `hostfwd=tcp::2222-:22`, `hostname` on serial equals the seed's value on the first boot, and `ssh -p 2222 ci@localhost edel settings export` succeeds; cargo test for the shadow line. Notes: apply never deletes users it did not create; password hashes live in the `/etc` overlay on `/data`, never in the file.
- [x] **3. `edel settings export`, `diff`, `set` and `reset`.** Export writes `settings.toml` plus the list of files in `/data/etc/upper`; diff is a dry run of apply and exits 0 when there is nothing to do, listing not-yet-installed apps as pending rather than drift; `edel settings set KEY=VALUE` edits one key in place with `toml_edit`, so comments, order and keys this slot does not know survive byte for byte (the compositor and Settings use it from M4 on); `set` refuses an unknown key or value it is asked to write, keeps and reports unknown keys already in the file, and on a newer-format file changes `settings.toml.v<N>` and says the change applies to this release only; `unset KEY` removes a key, and apply enforces the release default for every absent key it owns, taken from the defaults table in `edel::settings` filled from the slot (for a file in the `/etc` overlay such as `/etc/hostname`, by removing the upper copy); no writer ever writes a default; diff also lists ignored keys, unknown values and unknown names (ADR-008).
  Done when: system-test: apply, export, diff is empty; `edel settings set network.hostname=other` and diff shows exactly that one change; after `unset network.hostname` and apply, `hostname` prints the image's hostname; a line `future.key = 1` and a hand-written comment above `[users.ci]` survive the set and the unset byte for byte.
- [x] **4. `edel install DISK --settings FILE`.** Partitions the target with `boot.rs` Layout, copies the running slot's partition to slot A with a fresh UUID, writes the ESP from `/usr/lib/edel/boot` and an initial grubenv, creates `edel-data` and puts the settings file in it; refuses the disk it runs from. `sfdisk`, `dosfstools` and `mtools` join `images/vm.toml` (today they live only on the build host). Install builds one plan in `edel::settings` (the disk's model and size, and its partitions with labels and filesystems, such as `Windows (NTFS, 420 GB)`) and uses it three ways: `--dry-run` prints it and changes nothing, a terminal run prints it and asks the person to type the disk's name, and a run with neither a terminal nor `--yes` prints it and exits 3 (ADR-008).
  Done when: `ci/install-test.sh` boots the VM image with a blank second virtio disk, a test service (`command_background=yes` and a pidfile like the guard, waiting until `edel update status` shows the running slot confirmed, so the guard never waits on it) first runs `edel install /dev/vdb --settings /usr/share/edel/ci/install.toml` without `--yes` (serial shows the plan and exit code 3, vdb's sha256 is unchanged) and then with `--yes`, QEMU then boots vdb alone to the file's hostname at the login prompt with slot A confirmed and ssh host keys that differ from the live image's; a cargo test asserts the install plan uses only tools listed in the image definition. Notes: no ISO; the disk image written to a USB stick is the live medium and the installer; copying the running slot avoids carrying a payload inside a slot.
- [x] **5. Fresh install plus update plus rollback, required.** `ci.yml` runs install-test and then ab-test against the installed disk; docs say the job is required for merging (branch protection is a repository setting Alimardon turns on).
  Done when: a test branch that breaks a first-boot service fails the job. Notes: AerynOS lesson 3, enforced rather than remembered.

**Parts after M2:** unchanged, plus two data formats (image definition, settings file), both TOML with a format number. **Deliberately not added:** cloud-init or tiny-cloud, Ansible-style scripts, polkit, an ISO, a separate installer, a migration registry before a format has changed.

**Principles check:** Reliable (fresh installs tested like updates, checkers refuse what they do not know while boot readers keep it and say so, ADR-008); Simple (one data file for the machine and its shell, four commands, the installer is a command); Scalable comes free from the same file later.

## M3 (phase 1, now): Release train, kept small

**Goal:** every merge to main produces signed images Alimardon can download and try, on a VM or by writing `edel-laptop` to a USB stick; a tag produces a draft release that is kept forever; images carry a version, a platform level and the list of packages inside them; CI fails on size, memory and boot-time budgets; every kept release is upgraded to the PR's build in CI.

**Why now:** Alimardon needs a URL and a laptop image to try, budgets must exist before the desktop image grows, and the compatibility promise (ADR-005) needs old releases kept from the first one.

**Depends on:** M1, M2.

- [x] **1. Version, channel, platform level and package record inside the image.** `/usr/lib/os-release` gains `VERSION_ID` and `EDEL_CHANNEL`, `EDEL_ARCH`, `EDEL_LOADER_VERSION`, `EDEL_PLATFORM_LEVEL` next to the `EDEL_HEALTH` keys from M1.5; the version `YYYY.MM.N` comes from the runner (`git describe --tags` in ci.yml, passed as `EDEL_VERSION`; the build container has no git and the checkout is another uid's), so `edel image build` stays deterministic; the build writes the installed package list (`apk list --installed`) to `/usr/share/edel/packages` and `out/<image>.packages`; the PR summary shows the diff against the last preview, as a record, never a gate; `edel update status` shows running and available versions; every image of one CI run installs from one package snapshot, so images of one release cannot differ (Duranium does this).
  Done when: boot-test greps the version from serial; cargo tests for parsing and ordering; the diff appears in the step summary. Notes: apk has no lockfile and a true lock would need a mirror, a part we reject; the monthly rebuild in M8 is the bump.
- [x] **2. Budgets enforced.** `ci/budgets.toml` (container tar.gz MiB, VM slot used MiB, boot to the mark-good line in seconds read from `/proc/uptime` inside the guest, idle used memory MiB from `/proc/meminfo` printed on serial), first values from measurement plus 20 percent; `ci/sizes.sh` takes the median of three boots before comparing, fails the job when exceeded and writes the summary table.
  Done when: a test branch with a halved budget fails; main passes. Notes: tightening a budget is a reviewed change; a budget failure on an unrelated PR is retried once and otherwise filed, never silently relaxed.
- [x] **3a. One module list, a laptop image, its kernel checked.** `edel image build` owns one `modules=` list, the watchdog arguments and one mkinitfs features line (`ata base ext4 kms nvme scsi usb virtio`), the same for every bootable image, and rebuilds each initramfs with them; GRUB loads `intel-ucode.img` and `amd-ucode.img` before the initramfs when a slot carries them. `images/laptop.toml` = vm with linux-lts plus the firmware for most laptops' graphics and Wi-Fi (`linux-firmware-` amdgpu, ath10k, ath11k, i915, intel, mediatek, radeon, rtl_nic, rtlwifi, rtw88, rtw89, xe) and intel-ucode and amd-ucode; `ci/initramfs-check.sh` asserts the kernel options our steps rely on (overlay, the watchdog drivers, ext4, vfat, devtmpfs and more) from `ci/kernel-options.toml`, with a reason per option, as postmarketOS does, and lists the modules in each initramfs. The VM keeps linux-virt (see the notes).
  Done when: initramfs-check lists nvme, usb-storage, xhci_pci, i915 and amdgpu in the laptop initramfs; boot-test and ab-test pass on vm and boot-test passes on laptop; the sizes table shows both. Notes: linux-lts installs 152.4 MiB against linux-virt's 49.4 MiB (Alpine v3.24 index, 2026-10-02), more than the VM's whole 91 MiB root, a delta the budgets would show, so the VM keeps virt with the reason written in `vm.toml`, as this step allowed; virt has no softdog, so in a VM the guard pets QEMU's i6300esb. Alimardon's laptops are the only test of Wi-Fi and firmware coverage.
- [x] **3b. Slots of 4 GiB and `edel report`.** Every installed machine's slots are 4096 MiB (`edel::install::SLOT_MIB`), whatever it was installed from, and so are the VM image's; recorded in `docs/FORMATS.md` as a disk-layout decision (a preview machine must be able to take every later update). The laptop image keeps 1024 MiB slots so it fits a common 8 GB stick, and `edel install` copies its slot into a 4096 MiB one and grows it. The tests' disks and the A/B test's full-slot check grow to match. A tiny `edel report` prints the release, kernel, the boot line, memory in use, the PCI devices from sysfs with their drivers and dmesg as TOML the owner pastes into an issue.
  Done when: boot-test, install-test and ab-test pass with 4096 MiB slots; a cargo test parses the report's TOML; the system test runs `edel report` over ssh and parses it. Notes: numbers from laptops arrive through `edel report` once Alimardon names the reference laptop.
- [ ] **4. Release workflow, the preview, and releases kept forever.** `.github/workflows/release.yml`: a push to main uploads signed `release.toml`, `*.img.gz`, `*.ext4.gz`, `*.tar.gz` and `*.packages` to a `preview` pre-release updated in place, with `docs/TRY-IT.md` (dd `edel-laptop` to USB, the QEMU command for vm, "preview: no promise, data persistence as of M1", how to send `edel report`); a `v*` tag does the same into a draft release and updates `channels/stable/release.toml` on the `gh-pages` branch; keys from secret `EDEL_RELEASE_KEY`, public half committed. `docs/RELEASE.md` states that the assets of every tagged release are never deleted, and `ci/levels.toml` lists the supported releases with their image URLs and platform levels.
  Done when: the tag path runs end to end as a dry run and `edel update --check` with the preview URL prints the version on a VM. Notes: the first preview publish and the secret wait for Alimardon's yes; GitHub Pages holds only manifests (1 GB soft limit), images go to Releases. Before the first publish, M3.7 and M3.8 are done and one narrow review (Opus) of the updater, the guard and the signing path has run (Alimardon, 2026-10-06).
- [ ] **5. Upgrade test from every kept release.** `ci/upgrade-test.sh` downloads the VM image of every entry in `ci/levels.toml` (and the preview), updates each to this PR's build over the local HTTP server, boots it, confirms, and boots once more so the loader swap from M1.8 is exercised on the old ESP; skipped while no preview exists.
  Done when: PASS on every PR after the first preview, with the loader transition line on serial for each entry. Notes: this is the update real users run; no per-build test covers "from the bits people have", and nothing else exercises an old loader meeting a new slot.

- [x] **6. A desktop to try, before any publishing.** On every push to `main`, CI keeps `edel-desktop-x86_64.img.gz` and `edel-laptop-x86_64.img.gz`, the images it just booted, signed with the throwaway key, as a download (a workflow artifact kept 14 days); `docs/TRY-IT.md` says how to write one to a USB stick and start a laptop, and that it is for testing only. So the desktop meets real hardware before the release key exists: every PR so far was proven in a VM under software rendering (P1 of the 2026-10-06 review). Started from a stick, the desktop image makes a person who logs in by itself (`live`, no password, kept in the stick's `/etc` overlay and never in the slot; pulled forward from M6.6a), so the desktop shows without a login. TRY-IT also shows VirtualBox: the disk converted with `VBoxManage convertfromraw`, EFI turned on and the VMSVGA adapter, for which the desktop image carries `vmwgfx` and Mesa's `svga` driver (check in CI).
  Done when: the run on `main` lists the artifact with both images and their sizes, and TRY-IT's steps name it; a desktop-test case starts the desktop image as a removable USB disk (QEMU's `usb-storage` with `removable=on`, check in CI) and finds the panel with nobody logging in; initramfs-check lists `vmwgfx` in the desktop image. Notes: Alimardon's test laptop is an HP 250 G8 (Intel Iris Xe graphics), whose Wi-Fi, sleep and graphics the stick's report shows; nothing is rebuilt for the artifact. Done with: `edel boot live` (the login feature's `edel-live` service, in the boot runlevel) logs `live` in when the running disk is removable (on USB or marked removable, as `edel report --esp` decides) or when nobody has an account yet, the second so VirtualBox, whose disk is not removable, shows the desktop too; `live` is a system account, so the settings file, apply and export never hold it, and greetd takes `/run/edel/greetd.toml`, the slot's config with an `initial_session`, through `conf.d/greetd`; the person's session is one script, `/usr/libexec/edel-session`, which agreety and the live session both start; `vmwgfx` loads early with `virtio_gpu`; the artifact is `edel-try-x86_64`, with `SHA256SUMS`; `desktop-test live` boots the released image on `-vga std`, where Mesa draws with llvmpipe by itself, and runs in the VM test lane.
- [x] **7. Signing kept apart from the build.** Signing moves to a job of its own on a fresh runner, after the build job: it checks out the commit, downloads only `out/release` and `out/channel` from the build as an artifact, builds the signer from the locked sources (`Cargo.lock`'s checksums pin every crate) with nothing else installed, and signs; nothing the build produced runs while the key is present, and `ci/sign.sh` no longer runs `./target/release/edel` or `apk add` (R2 of the 2026-10-06 review). The job runs in a GitHub environment named `release`; making that environment wait for Alimardon's approval is a repository setting, which waits for them.
  Done when: a PR run shows the sign job signing the build's release with the throwaway key and `edel release verify` passing on its output. Notes: a hardware key or a key service comes with M11. Done with: the build job uploads only the two lists (`edel-lists`) and checks with `ci/keys-carried.sh` that its images carry the release's public keys; the `sign` job builds `edel` with `cargo build --release --locked`, uncached, signs and verifies, and hands `edel-signed` to the publish job; `ci/release.sh dry-run` and `ci/channel.sh dry-run` check the lists without their signatures, while `publish` still needs them; the environment applies to pushes only, so pull requests never wait for an approval.
- [x] **8. Manifests expire and name their channel.** `release.toml` gains `expires`, a date, and its `channel` is checked: `edel update` refuses an expired list ("this release list expired on DATE: the server may be stale, or replaying an old list") and a list for another channel than the machine's (`system.channel`, `stable` unless set; preview machines name `preview`) unless asked with `--channel`, and accepts only `https://` and file paths (`--allow-http` for CI's local server). Older updaters ignore the new field, as M1.6's reader does, so this is no format bump; the signing step sets the date (R3 of the 2026-10-06 review, ADR-009).
  Done when: cargo tests (an expired list refused, another channel refused, `http://` refused without the flag, a list without `expires` from an older release still read); ab-test refuses an expired list (`refused: expired` on serial) before installing the good one. Notes: downgrades are already refused (M1.6), so this closes a frozen or crossed list, not a replay of an older release. Done with: the machine's channel is `updates.channel` (supported now), else the image's own `EDEL_CHANNEL`, so a preview image follows preview by itself; `expires` is a day, `YYYY-MM-DD` in UTC, set by `edel release sign --expires-in 60`; the expired message also asks to check the clock (M1.13).
- [x] **9. A dependency audit on every PR.** `cargo audit` (or `cargo deny`, chosen by run time in the PR) in the Rust checks job: a known vulnerability fails the PR, an unmaintained crate is listed in the summary, and exceptions live in one file with a reason and a date each. The two quick-xml advisories it finds today (RUSTSEC-2026-0194 and RUSTSEC-2026-0195, through the accessibility stack) are fixed or listed with their reason in the same PR; cgmath (unmaintained and unsound, through smithay) and ttf-parser (unmaintained) are listed. The workflow's actions and the `alpine:3.24` image are pinned by digest (R9 of the 2026-10-06 review).
  Done when: the check runs on every PR, and a test branch pinning a crate with a known advisory fails it. Done with: `cargo audit` (cargo-deny not measured: the disk was full when it was tried), its policy in `ci/audit.sh` and the accepted advisories with reasons and dates in `ci/audit-exceptions.toml`; the two quick-xml advisories stay accepted, as their fix (zbus_xml 5.2.1) needs Rust 1.87 and the crates say 1.85, and the code reads only bundled XML at build time (from reading the source); a crate pinning smallvec 1.6.0 failed the audit locally with RUSTSEC-2021-0003; the Alpine image's digest lives in `features/base.toml` beside its branch.

**Parts after M3:** unchanged in the image; in CI: GitHub Releases and Pages. **Deliberately not added:** an apk repository, a package lock, a build farm, OBS, a mirror network, a second channel, a website, arm64 (M9).

**Principles check:** Reliable (signed delivery, the upgrade test from every kept release, the loader transition tested); Efficient (budgets before the desktop image exists); Simple kept GitHub as the only host, one kernel and a record instead of a lock gate.

## M4 (next): The compositor

**Goal:** the desktop image boots in QEMU with a virtual GPU into our compositor: windows float with compositor-drawn title bars (close and maximize; minimize joins with M5.2's window list), one key or one line in `settings.toml` switches a workspace to dynamic tiling with the title bars kept, two outputs and fractional scaling work, X11 apps run with the same title bars, a broken compositor update falls back, and CI uploads a screenshot. Alimardon boots the preview on a laptop and moves windows.

**Why now:** the largest and riskiest part; its CI harness (virtual GPU, screenshots, frame budget) is what every shell step is proven with, so it is built first and alone.

**Depends on:** M3.

- [x] **0. Images are made of feature files (ADR-008).** Numbered 0 so later references keep their numbers. `features/NAME.toml` (format = 1: `summary`; `why`, required when the feature adds a package or service; `packages`; `services` by runlevel; `modules` and `initramfs`, its kernel modules for `modules=` and its mkinitfs features; `switchable`; `addon`; `health`, the sentinels it writes; `flatpak` and `flatpak_dropped`, for the `apps` feature only), with `features/NAME/`, if present, copied over the root as `images/files/*` is today. Image definitions move to format 2: `features = [...]`, `off = [...]` (switchable features shipped with their services off, written as `EDEL_SERVICES_OFF` in os-release) and image facts only (name, variant, arch, hostname, `[alpine]`, `[vm]` without `modules=`, `[image] health_timeout`, `[release]`); format 1 is refused, since every definition lives in this repository and is converted here. `edel image build` merges the listed features in order (packages, services, modules and initramfs features as sets, so several features may list `dbus`), refuses an unknown feature, a missing `why`, one service in two runlevels, a file path shipped by two features and an `off` entry that is not switchable, writes the union of `health` into `EDEL_HEALTH`, and copies each feature file to `/usr/share/edel/features/NAME.toml`; `edel image check` checks `features/*.toml` strictly, while readers on a machine read them leniently. Container, vm, laptop (with `off = ["ssh"]`) and the CI definitions are converted, with `overlay` in `base` and `i6300esb` and `softdog` in `ab-boot` (other names settled in the PR); `docs/FEATURES.md` is the maintainer's how-to with ADR-008's worked examples.
  Done when: a cargo test loads main's format-1 definitions as fixtures and the merged format-2 result has the same `packages.install` set, the same `services.entries()` (the laptop's minus `sshd`), the same `modules=` and the same mkinitfs features; the M3.1 package diff against the preview is empty in the PR summary; cargo tests cover merge order, each refusal, a service shared by two features merged once, removing `ssh` from vm's list losing exactly `sshd` and `openssh-server`, and a feature file with an unknown field refused by `image check` and read with a report at runtime; `EDEL_HEALTH` is unchanged and boot-test, ab-test, install-test and upgrade-test pass. Notes: placed before `desktop.toml` exists, so the big image is born as a feature list; no dependencies, versions, scripts or alternatives between features (apk resolves packages); a package one image needs alone goes into a feature named after that image. Done in PR #36 with the features `base`, `container`, `machine`, `ab-boot`, `ssh`, `vm` and `laptop` (and CI's `flatpak-test` beside `ci/flatpak/vm.toml`): no preview existed yet (publishing waits for Alimardon, M3.4), so the package lists were compared with `main`'s last CI run instead, and upgrade-test does not exist yet (M3.5 is skipped).
- [x] **1. Desktop image and the CI display harness.** `images/desktop.toml` (variant `vm`: a bootable A/B disk like the laptop's, and a `desktop` variant would change nothing in the build), the laptop's features with `udev` (eudev with udev-init-scripts) instead of `mdev`, both split out of `machine`, plus `graphics` (mesa-dri-gallium, mesa-egl, mesa-gbm, mesa-vulkan-intel and -ati, mesa-va-gallium, libdrm, libinput, xkeyboard-config; `modules=` adds `virtio_gpu`), `seat` (seatd under supervise-daemon; the `edel-rundir` boot service makes `/run/edel/session/`, mode 1770, group `seat`, for the files root reads, `ready` and `state.toml`, while `/run/edel/` stays root's alone), `login` (greetd under supervise-daemon with agreety on VT 7; `pam-rundir` with `session optional pam_rundir.so` in `/etc/pam.d/greetd`, so every session gets `/run/user/UID` (0700) without logind), `fonts` (font-inter, and font-noto for Noto Sans Mono, since foot warned that Inter is not monospace), `terminal` (foot) and `sway-baseline` (Alpine's `sway`, for one baseline only), with `off = ["ssh"]` and 1536 MiB slots; dbus arrives in M4.2b with its first user. CI-only `ci/desktop/vm.toml` adds the `desktop-test` feature (weston-clients; greetd's `initial_session` logs user ci in to sway; a session script measures); `ci/desktop-test.sh CASE...` boots it once, alone after the other VM tests, with `-m 2048 -smp 4`, `virtio-vga`, `virtio-keyboard-pci`, `virtio-tablet-pci` and a QMP socket as its own QEMU arguments (`ci/vm.sh` is unchanged, and the vm and server tests keep 512 MiB so their budget stays honest); `ci/qmp.py` (python3 stdlib only) does screendump to PNG (QEMU 7.1+; ubuntu-24.04 ships 8.2), click, key, type and pixel reads; first cases `console` and `baseline` (sway autologged in, its RSS, boot-to-ready and frame p99 under llvmpipe, from the time between frames `weston-presentation-shm` sees presented, recorded as the baseline the first budgets are set from).
  Done when: `desktop-test console` boots to the login prompt on serial, `/run/user/<uid>` exists with mode 0700 after autologin, the screendump is not uniformly black and is uploaded as an artifact; `baseline` writes its numbers into the step summary. Notes: `-display none` still renders into the virtual framebuffer; `mkinitfs.conf` must be in place before the kernel package's trigger runs (copy files before `apk add`, or rerun mkinitfs); sway leaves the image once the compositor exists. `console` also finds foot's title bar colour on the screenshot and types `exit` into foot, which must close it, so the keyboard path is proven from the first step. Mesa has no 3D driver for virtio-vga ("virtio_gpu: driver missing" and sway exits), so CI's session sets `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`, which draws with llvmpipe into the GPU's buffers, with `WLR_RENDERER=gles2` and `WLR_RENDERER_ALLOW_SOFTWARE=1`; virtio-gpu copies a buffer to QEMU when it is flipped and nothing makes the flip wait for llvmpipe's threads, so screenshots showed half-drawn frames until `LP_NUM_THREADS=0` made llvmpipe finish first; seatd starts before greetd (`rc_before` in its conf.d), or the first session can start before its seat. The definition is a feature list (M4.0): the laptop's features plus `graphics` (which carries `virtio_gpu`), `seat`, `login`, `fonts`, `terminal` and `sway-baseline`, with `off = ["ssh"]`, so dropping sway is deleting one line.
- [x] **2a. Floating windows and a deterministic test client.** `layout::Floating`, the first `WindowPolicy` (which gains `moved`): a window opens once its first buffer comes, centred at the size it drew (two thirds of the area if it lets the compositor choose), 32 px down and right of an open window whose centre it would share, and inside the area, then stays where it is put; a new window takes the keyboard, a click focuses and raises, and a closed window hands the keyboard to the one on top; one pointer grab (`grabs.rs`) moves or resizes, started by the window's own move and resize requests (its title bar and edges) or by Super with the left button (move) or the right button (resize from the nearest corner), never smaller than 96x64; `crates/testclient` (`edel-testclient`, smithay-client-toolkit `=0.21.1` without calloop or xkbcommon) draws one solid-colour window of a given size, colour and title, and CI's `desktop-test` feature ships it through `programs`; the compositor writes `/run/edel/session/state.toml` (`format`, `policy`, `[[displays]]` and `[[windows]]` with title, app_id, place, size and focus, bottom of the stack first) from a thread of its own on map, unmap, the end of a move or resize and output changes, never during a drag (no disk on the main loop, ADR-002), creating its new file only under a name nothing else has, so no link planted in the shared directory is followed.

Done with these changes: the files are watched with inotify on the compositor's own event loop instead of the `notify` crate, as the "Shell, outputs and update settings" row already said, which adds no thread and two small crates instead of a watcher stack; the directories are watched, since writers replace the file through a rename, and the person's `~/.config/edel/` is made if missing so a file written there later is seen; "over serial" is a second serial port of the desktop VM on which its test service runs the commands `desktop-test.sh` sends (`tiling on`), as root; each workspace holds every policy, so back in floating each window is where it floated; Super+T switches the workspace and leaves the file alone, while a change of `layout.tiling` in either file switches it again; a tiled window hears it is tiled, so apps that draw their own frames drop their shadows; maximize still works in tiling, over the tiles. `desktop-test tiling` sets `layout.tiling=true` in the VM, finds foot and one side by side, not overlapping, each with its bar, then presses Super+T and finds them back where they floated.

Notes: a crash still loses the session; the point is never leaving a black screen, and the Hyprland complaint (no close button) must not return through X11.

  - [x] **7a. XWayland.** X11 apps through XWayland, started on demand, with the same title bar. Done when: `xclock` shows the title-bar row in the token colour and a `qmp.py click` on its X is followed by `unmapped window xclock`. Done with: xwayland-satellite (Alpine's `xwayland-satellite` 0.8.1, 2.9 MiB) instead of smithay's XWayland support, because smithay 0.7 opens XWayland's sockets itself and so cannot start it on demand, while an XWayland started with every session cost about 45 MiB of memory (measured locally under llvmpipe), more than the desktop budget had left after M4.6c, and budgets are never raised to make a change pass. The compositor holds the X11 display's sockets and starts satellite, which runs XWayland on them, when the first X11 app connects; satellite turns X11 windows into ordinary Wayland windows, so they get our bars (it asks for them through xdg-decoration unless the window draws its own), policies and keyboard focus, and an X11 failure cannot take the desktop down. If satellite stops, the next X11 app starts it again. The `xwayland` feature brings both packages, so an image without it has no X11 apps. `xclock` is in Alpine (44 KiB, CI's image only). `desktop-test xwayland` checks that no X11 process runs before xclock, xclock's bar in the focused colour and its close button, and reports what XWayland and satellite use.
  - [x] **7b. Respawn.** greetd owns the compositor's lifecycle, no supervise-daemon around the compositor itself. Done when: `kill -9 edel-compositor` is followed by greetd's `default_session` printing a new ready line within 5 s. Done with: the greeter (`default_session`) is our compositor running agreety in foot, `edel-compositor -- foot agreety --cmd 'dbus-run-session -- edel-compositor'`, since agreety alone prints no ready line and M4.8's health means "the login screen is up"; `edel-compositor -- PROGRAM ARG...` runs one program once the desktop is on screen and stops when it exits, so logging in ends the greeter's compositor before greetd starts the person's; `/run/edel/session` loses its sticky bit (0770, group `seat`) and the health file is replaced by a rename, like the state file, because the greeter (user greetd) and the person each write `ready` and `state.toml` in turn. `desktop-test respawn` kills the session's compositor and checks the greeter's ready line, its time, the health file's owner and the greeter's foot window, then logs ci in through agreety (CI's image alone gives ci a password) and checks that ci's compositor takes the health file back, the first test of logging in through the greeter.
- [x] **8. A dead desktop rolls back.** The `compositor` feature declares `health = ["compositor"]` and `ab-boot` declares `default-runlevel`, and `edel image build` writes their union into `EDEL_HEALTH` (M4.0), so `edel boot guard` also waits for `/run/edel/session/ready`, written by whichever compositor greetd starts, the greeter's included: health means "the login screen is up", not "a user is in".
  Done when: a desktop ab-test variant installs an update whose compositor binary is broken and the VM comes back on the previous slot. Notes: closes ADR-006's "the desktop fails to start" promise. Until M5.10 the greeter is agreety, which writes no ready file, so a desktop waiting at the login prompt would never be healthy: this step either brings the compositor as greeter forward or counts greetd's greeter as up (M4.2b). Done with: M4.7b made the greeter our compositor, so the greeter's first frame is the health; a compositor that finds no connected screen at start (unplugged, or off) stays up, writes its health file as `no screen connected yet; waiting for one` and lights the screen once udev says one came, so a desktop booted without its screen is not restarted by the guard and rolled back for want of a frame; the "desktop ab-test variant" is `desktop-test.sh rollback`, in the VM test lane next to the A/B test: CI's desktop image takes CI's first key and a 30 s health timeout, `ci/build.sh` signs its own update image, the VM installs it into slot B from the host's port 8001, the test service puts a compositor that only fails there and gives slot B its last try, and slot B, never healthy, is restarted by the watchdog and switched off, so slot A comes back and says so.

**Parts after M4:** plus the compositor (one process) and one data format, feature files (M4.0), which also takes the place of the add-on format M7.2 would have added; reused greetd, seatd, eudev, Mesa, XWayland, foot. **Deliberately not added:** a window-manager plugin or extension API, a second renderer, a session manager script, an IPC daemon, a settings daemon, golden-image tests.

**Principles check:** Reliable (rollback covers the desktop, greetd respawns); Instant (damage tracking proven by `idle_frames = 0`, a frame budget measured against sway from the first PR); Simple (one file watched, one trait, one supervisor, a test client instead of goldens). Beautiful waits for tokens in M5.

## M5 (next): shell-ui, presets, settings, tokens

**Goal:** Classic out of the box; one button in Settings (or `edel settings set layout.preset=mac-like`) switches to Mac-like, Windows-like or Hive (tiling) and everything re-lays out live; launcher, Alt-Tab, workspaces, tray, notifications, quick settings, lock screen and login work; the settings app is the first adaptive app; Appearance and Shortcuts pages let a user change wallpaper, colours, fonts, sizes and keys without editing a file; one token file styles the compositor, shell-ui and GTK apps; effect tiers keep frames on time; CI uploads one screenshot per preset for Alimardon to review.

**Depends on:** M4. **Order (2026-10-06):** steps 25, 27, 26 and 19 to 24, in that order after M3.6, come before step 6, with the safety steps M1.10 to M1.13 and M3.7 to M3.9 (ADR-009).

A widget is one module plus one line in shell-ui's static widget table; code that talks to an OS feature lives in `crates/shell-ui/src/features/NAME.rs` and its line carries `needs = "NAME"`, so shell-ui skips it with a log line when `/usr/share/edel/features/NAME.toml` is absent; cargo tests fail when a module names a feature with no file under `features/` and when a preset names a widget the table lacks (ADR-008).

The design tokens move to `edel::tokens` so the compositor and shell-ui read one file with one reader, and gain `colour.panel`, `colour.panel_text`, `size.panel`, `size.panel_text` and `size.radius`. Text uses one shaping library for every script (cosmic-text by default: shaping, fallback fonts and right-to-left in one crate; check its weight in 1b); the compositor's title bars may move to it later. Done with: `notify.rs` (`org.freedesktop.Notifications` served on the connection of the portal and the tray, no connection and no thread of its own, about 24 KiB of anonymous memory when served on an existing connection and nothing more until an app calls; `Notify`, `CloseNotification`, `GetCapabilities` with `body`, `actions` and `persistence`, `GetServerInformation` as `Edel`, and the `NotificationClosed` and `ActionInvoked` signals; text only, at most 50 in the list; a banner that goes away is not a closed notification, as `persistence` says); `notice.rs`, `banner.rs`, `calendar.rs` and `centre.rs` (the banner and the centre, each laid out by one function, `banner::layout` and `centre::layout`, drawn from the tokens and the shell's icons, with AccessKit nodes that say "Notification from APP: SUMMARY, BODY", 44 px targets at Compact width, and previews from `EDEL_NOTIFY_PNG=DIR cargo test -p edel-shell-ui the_banner_and_the_centre_draw`); `notify_card.rs` (the surfaces, which exist only while shown; the banner stays 5 s, the app's own time, or until clicked when critical, and is held while the pointer is over it); the key `notifications.do_not_disturb` (format 1, `keys.txt`, `docs/settings.md`), Settings' Notifications page with its one row, quick settings' `do_not_disturb` tile after `airplane` in every preset (a plain toggle, no arrow) and the centre's switch row, which all write the one line of the person's file as Settings does, and `edel settings set notifications.do_not_disturb=maybe` is refused with `expected true or false, not "maybe"`; the clock opens the centre and is lit while it is open; desktop-test's `notify` case sends its notifications with `gdbus call`, as libnotify is not in the image. Decisions: a banner's close button dismisses the notification (it leaves the list and the app hears `NotificationClosed`, reason 2), as the centre's does; a click on a body with no `default` action only hides the banner; a new notification replaces the banner showing, the older one stays listed; the centre shows as many whole cards as fit 250 px and the wheel scrolls the rest.

- [x] **16. Tiling styles, chosen from the layout button or in Settings.** Tiling lays windows out in one of three styles, set by one key, `layout.tiling_style` (ADR-002's tiling styles decision of 2026-10-04): `stack`, today's tiling and the default, one main window on the left and the rest stacked on the right; `split`, as Hyprland tiles by default, each new window taking half of the focused window's space, cut across its longer side, and a closed window giving its space back to its neighbour; `scroll`, as niri tiles, each window a column on a strip that may be wider than the screen, a new window opening as a column beside the focused one, and the strip scrolling so the focused column is always whole on screen, so windows keep their size however many are open. Super+T still switches a workspace between floating and tiling; the style says how tiling lays the windows out, and title bars with their close button stay in every style. A right click on the layout button, or a long press with touch or a pen, opens a small menu of the three styles, each with its picture and the chosen one marked; choosing one writes `layout.tiling_style` to the person's settings file with the table `edel settings set` writes, and changes nothing else: a floating workspace stays floating and takes the style when it next tiles (Alimardon, 2026-10-04). Settings' Layout page (M5.6) offers the same three and writes the same key, so the menu, Settings and `edel settings set layout.tiling_style=scroll` each show what the others chose. Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-04. Split into three parts, ticked as each lands:
  - [x] **16a. The key, the split style and the arrows.** (Split on 2026-10-07 into 16a, 16b the menu and 16c the scroll style, so each is one PR.) `layout.tiling_style` (`stack` or `split`; absent means `stack`) joins format 1 of the settings file (a new key is no format bump; `keys.txt` gains its line) and is read leniently like every enum key, so a style a release lacks falls back to `stack` and is reported; `scroll` joins its values with 16c. `split` is one module beside `tiling.rs`, `split.rs`, and one line in tiling's `Style` table, as `stack` is, both behind the one tiling policy, so Super+T and `layout.tiling` still switch only between floating and tiling. The compositor, which already watches the file, applies the style live (`edel-compositor: tiling style split`), and a new window halves the focused one. Super with an arrow key moves the focus to the window in that direction in every policy, and Super+Shift with an arrow key swaps the focused window with that one when the workspace tiles; both are new actions in the shortcut table (M5.13a). Settings' Layout page has the Tiling style row, after Tile windows. Done with: cargo tests for split's places with 1 to 6 windows on three screens, for each window closed its space going to its neighbour, for the arrows' window in line, and the compositor's styles held to the key's values; desktop-test's `styles` case sets `layout.tiling_style=split` with workspace 4 floating and sees four windows still float, Super+T tiles them as split, the fourth sharing the bottom right quarter with the third, side by side, Super+Shift+Left swaps the fourth with the third, and `stack` stacks them on the right again, live.

- [ ] **17. Your own presets.** A person changes the desktop to their liking, saves it as a preset of their own under a name, and picks it again later the one way every preset is picked, `layout.preset` (ADR-008's own presets decision of 2026-10-04): a card on Settings' Layout page, `edel settings set layout.preset=NAME` or one line in the file. An own preset is a `[presets.NAME]` table in the settings file holding the same keys as `[layout]`, with `preset` naming the built-in it starts from: `[presets.mine]` with `preset = "hive"` and `window_buttons = "left"` is Hive with the buttons on the left. So the one file that copies a machine carries it, and a fleet's file can give every machine the same one. Saving is Save as: Settings' Save as preset button, or `edel preset save NAME`, moves the `[layout]` keys a preset holds into `[presets.NAME]` and leaves `[layout]` with `preset = "NAME"`, so nothing on screen changes and picking Classic afterwards gives plain Classic; saving while on an own preset starts the new one from the same built-in. `edel preset delete NAME` (Settings' Delete) removes the table and refuses the preset in use, saying to pick another first; `edel preset list` shows the built-in presets, then the person's, marking the one in use. A preset holds the layout only: appearance and shortcuts stay as they are when the preset changes. An own preset may not take a built-in's name, and save says so. Read leniently like the rest of the file: a key this release lacks is left out, and a base preset it lacks is Classic, each shown by `edel settings diff`. Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-04.
  Done when: cargo tests: save moves exactly the layout keys into `[presets.NAME]`, keeps every other byte of the file, and the preset it saves resolves to the same layout as before saving; save refuses a built-in's name, delete refuses the preset in use; a table whose base this release lacks gives Classic and a report. desktop-test sets Hive and `layout.window_buttons=left`, runs `edel preset save mine` and sees nothing move, sets `layout.preset=classic` and sees Classic's panel along the bottom with the buttons on the right, then sets `layout.preset=mine` and sees Hive's bar on top with the buttons on the left again; a screendump of the Layout page shows `mine` after the built-in presets. Notes: level 1, settings (ADR-007): an own preset is part of the person's settings file, added by saving and removed by deleting it, and copying the file copies it. It comes after the Layout page (M5.6) and the panels as a setting (M5.4e), so a saved preset can hold its panels as `[[presets.NAME.panels]]`.

Notes: direct scanout of fullscreen dmabufs comes with game mode (M7.7). Done with: `dmabuf.rs`, smithay's `DmabufState` with a version 4 global and default feedback on the GPU's render node (else its primary node), offering the formats `GlesRenderer::dmabuf_formats` lists; each buffer is checked by importing it once (`Importer`, a closure over the DRM backend's renderer), and the first window that hands one over is logged; with no format to offer the compositor says so and apps keep shared memory; the winit development backend offers none. In CI the desktop runs on llvmpipe, so `desktop-test dmabuf` checks the global and its formats; the GL client on virgl waits for the reference laptop's `edel report`, as the step allows.

The file and the command already do it (M5.4e: `[[layout.panels]]`, any edge, any widgets in any order at the start, centre or end, a bar or a dock, a dock that hides); this step gives everyone the same control by hand, run right after M5.9 so its status widgets are there to place. Right-click a panel, or the Layout page's Panels group, and choose Edit panels: every panel shows its widgets as tiles with a soft outline, a drawer shows every widget the release has (each with its name and a small picture, the ones already placed dimmed), and a person drags a widget along a panel, from one panel to another, from the drawer onto a panel or off it to remove it; a panel itself drags to another edge; each panel has a small menu with Bar or Dock, Size (small, medium, large, from the tokens), Hide when a window covers it, Floating (a gap round it and rounded corners, as the dock), Show on every screen or the main one, Add a panel and Remove this panel; the dock's pinned apps drag the same way (`apps.pinned`). Done is one button, and Undo takes everything back, as on the Layout page (M5.6a). Every change writes `layout.panels` (and `apps.pinned`) with `edel::settings::set`, so the panels are still one line of the settings file and one command, `edel settings set layout.panels=...`, and Save as (M5.17) turns them into a preset of one's own. The new fields of a panel table, `size`, `floating` and `screens`, are checked by `edel::presets::check_panels` as every field is, and a preset may set them too; the absence of a field is the preset's choice, as always. No widget gains options of its own here: what a widget shows is a setting of its page (the clock's date follows `region`, the tray its pins), so there stays one place for each choice. At Compact width and on touch the same editor works with long-press instead of right-click and 44 px handles (the phone rule). Beyond KDE and Cinnamon: the whole arrangement is one line of the one file, so it moves to every machine a person has (M7.10f) and fleets set it for everyone (M7.3); it is undone in one click; and nothing new runs, as the editor is shell-ui's own surfaces.
