**Parts after M5:** compositor, shell-ui (also the greeter and the Settings portal backend), settings, `edel`; reused PipeWire, NetworkManager, BlueZ, UPower, xdg-desktop-portal. **Deliberately not added:** a dock process, a notification daemon, a separate greeter, a settings daemon, a portal backend process, dconf, a GTK theme fork, an extension API.

**Principles check:** Instant (tiers, frame budget on every PR); Simple (one file plus one tiny protocol, two processes); Beautiful (one token set reaches GTK through the portal); Reliable (the user is told about a fallback); Versatile served by preset files, not options.

## M6 (next): Apps, the basics, the installer page

**Goal:** a USB stick written from the desktop image boots live with a browser, a file manager, an editor and a store already there, offline; a user installs Firefox from the store without polkit; file chooser, screenshot and screen-sharing portals work; the everyday basics are a CI table with an honest "needs hardware" column; input methods and a screen reader path exist; default apps, startup apps, power actions and optional services are settings; the stick installs Edel OS from a page in Settings.

**Depends on:** M5, M2.

- [ ] **1. Flatpak and portals in the desktop image.** flatpak, bubblewrap, xdg-desktop-portal-gtk; `edel settings apply` adds Flathub per admin user (`flatpak --user`, SPIKE-flatpak) and installs `[apps] flatpak` there, in the background, never from the boot service.
  Done when: desktop-test installs a small app (from Flathub, or a local repo built with flatpak-builder on the runner if downloads are too slow) and its window maps; `edel settings diff` is empty afterwards and `pgrep polkitd` is empty.
- [ ] **2. Default app set inside the slot.** The out-of-box list (browser, files, text editor, store; names to check) shipped as a read-only Flatpak system installation in the slot: `/etc/flatpak/installations.d/edel.conf` pointing at `/usr/lib/edel/flatpak`, populated at `edel image build` from a cached pull; its size added to the budgets table; Flathub stays the user-visible remote for everything else. The set is the `apps` feature (`flatpak = [...]`); an id dropped from it moves to `flatpak_dropped`, and `edel settings apply` (network phase) adds a dropped id to `[apps] flatpak` of each user whose `~/.var/app/<id>` exists, so swapping a default app never takes one away (ADR-008).
  Done when: a VM booted with `-netdev user,restrict=on` lists the browser in the launcher and it maps; a CI definition that swaps test app A for B (local flatpak-builder repos) keeps A for a user with `~/.var/app/<A>` and gives a fresh user only B.
- [ ] **3. Screenshots and screen sharing.** The compositor adds wlr-screencopy and xdg-output; xdg-desktop-portal-wlr (check Alpine) as the backend, with `grim` and `slurp` in the image because wlr's Screenshot runs them.
  Done when: `gdbus call` on `org.freedesktop.portal.Screenshot` returns a file in `/home` and ScreenCast returns a stream. Notes: our own portal code only if wlr's protocol set blocks us. Seam (ADR-011): built only on the standard portals and PipeWire, so help (M7.10k) and a device as a screen (M7.10e) reuse it.
- [ ] **4. Store front and web apps.** Bazaar installed as a Flatpak (`io.github.kolunmi.Bazaar`, check), pinned in the Classic panel, installing into the user installation; the launcher's "install as app" writes a `.desktop` that runs the browser in app mode, so a web app has its own window and switcher entry.
  Done when: the store launches from the launcher, installs an app with `pgrep polkitd` empty; an installed web app appears in the launcher and maps as its own toplevel. Notes: GNOME Software and Discover are too heavy for the budget; AppStream device badges come in M10.
- [ ] **5. Privilege for system changes.** doas rules let admins run `edel` for settings without a password, but ask for the admin's own password before `edel install`, an update or rollback, and anything that adds ssh keys for root; `--unsigned` works only in developer mode (R6 of the 2026-10-06 review). Settings calls `doas edel ...` for the system-level file; how it asks for the password (a small prompt of shell-ui's, or polkit if NetworkManager already brought it) is decided in this step; `edel` refuses non-admin users; the Updates page gets its install button.
  Done when: system-test runs `doas edel status` as the admin user and a non-admin is refused. Notes: polkit only if NetworkManager already dragged it in (checked in M5 step 8: it did not; M5.8a runs NetworkManager with `auth-polkit=false` and a D-Bus policy for group `seat`).
- [ ] **6a. Installer page in Settings, one question list.** Shown when `/` is on removable media. The questions live once in `edel::settings`, in this order: language (it suggests keyboard and timezone), keyboard (applied live before any password is typed; a non-Latin layout gets `us` as a second layout), name and password (or an ssh key on vm and server), disk; timezone prefilled (`[region]`, applied by the Region page from here); under More options hostname (prefilled as user and form factor, such as `ali-laptop`), preset (Classic) and profile (none). `edel install --list` prints every candidate disk (model, size, partitions with labels and filesystems, removable, the boot medium marked) with M2.4's planner; the page shows that list and then the plan from `edel install DISK --plan` word for word; its Erase and install button stands for typing the disk's name, and only then does Settings write a `settings.toml` holding only the answers and run `doas edel install DISK --settings FILE --yes`. The live session's user is created at boot only when `/` is on removable media, in the stick's `/etc` overlay, never in the slot (ADR-008).
  Done when: `install-test gui` clicks through the page with `qmp.py` to install on vdb; the rebooted disk reaches the greeter, its file holds no key left at its default, its `/etc/passwd` has no live user, and ssh to it is refused; cargo tests for the `--list` rule. Notes: no separate installer app; AerynOS lesson 4.
- [ ] **6b. Install from a terminal, a console or a settings file.** The same question list on a terminal: `edel install` without `--system` asks it, and without DISK it shows the `--list` output and asks which disk; on an image with no user and no seed, `edel settings first-boot --console`, run from the console lines of `/etc/inittab` (after `openrc default`, so the guard never waits for a person), asks for a user with a password or ssh key and then execs getty; no image ships a default password. A settings file comes from a path or an `https://` URL (`--system`, `edel settings apply`, and a Use a settings file choice on the page that skips every question the file answers and asks only for what is missing); `http://` is refused, since the file is unsigned and creates admins and ssh keys. `ci/vm.sh` gains a serial socket (`-serial unix:...,server,nowait`) and `ci/serial.py`, which types into it. `docs/INSTALL.md` lists every way in, each ending in one `settings.toml`: `dd` with the sha256 and signature check on Linux; on Windows and macOS only stick writers CI or Alimardon has tried, with the sha256 check (`certutil -hashfile`, `shasum -a 256`) against the download page's value from the signed manifest; turning Secure Boot off and the common boot-menu keys (ADR-008).
  Done when: `install-test tty` types the answers over the serial console into `edel install` and the installed file holds the answered keys and none left at its default; `install-test url` installs with `--settings https://10.0.2.2:8443/install.toml`, served by python's `ssl` module with a throwaway CA only the CI definition trusts, and the same file over `http://` is refused; `install-test gui` with a second vfat disk holding `install.toml` installs through Use a settings file and the disk boots to its hostname; a seedless vm answered over the serial socket reaches a login prompt and the new user logs in over ssh.

Notes: level 1; a person's own palette is M5.12's appearance tokens; no third-party theme format (ADR-007).
- [ ] **14. What each app may use, in Settings.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07, after the review of HarmonyOS and Android ([REVIEW-apps-and-lessons.md](REVIEW-apps-and-lessons.md)): an Apps page in Settings lists every app and what it may use (camera, microphone, location, files, screen sharing, notifications, running in the background), with Allow, Ask every time and Never for each, read from and written to Flatpak's own permission store and overrides, so there is no store of our own. Each choice is a line of the settings file under the Settings app's names (ADR-008), so one file gives a whole fleet the same permissions, `edel settings set` changes one, and `edel settings get` shows which app may use what. Beyond what Android and HarmonyOS offer: the panel shows a small sign while an app uses the camera, the microphone, the screen or the location, and a click on it names the app and stops it at once; Settings keeps a local record of which app used what and when (never sent anywhere, as there is no telemetry); permissions travel with the settings file to every machine.
  Done when: desktop-test opens the Apps page, sets the test app's camera to Never, `flatpak permission-show` and `flatpak override --show` agree, the settings file holds the line, and `edel settings reset` of it brings Ask back; a test client that opens the camera through the portal shows the panel's sign in the screendump. Notes: ADR-007 level 1 (settings); reuses Flatpak's permission store and the portals, adds no daemon; apps outside Flatpak are listed as "not sandboxed".
- [ ] **15. Laptop sound that works and sounds right.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-08, after asking why Linux sound has a bad name: much of it is per laptop (ADR-012). Speaker tuning profiles, PipeWire filter-chain files of a few kilobytes each, ship in the base for the laptops we can test, starting with the reference laptop, and WirePlumber loads the one matching the machine's DMI model at the start of the session; `firmware-sof`, Intel's sound processor firmware, becomes the first hardware pack (M7.11), on the always list instead if the reference laptop needs it to make any sound; the defaults favour quality and low delay (the device's own sample rate, no needless resampling); and "no sound" explains itself: Settings' Sound page and `edel status` say which firmware or profile is missing and what brings it. Beyond Windows and macOS: the tuning is open files any person can read and improve for their own model, sent back as a pull request, so one person's fix reaches everyone with that laptop.
  Done when: a cargo test matches sample DMI names to their profiles and to none; desktop-test's `sound` case, its VM given a test model name, finds the matching filter-chain node in `pw-dump`; `edel status` names a missing pack for a test device. Notes: profiles are matched in the base, never downloaded (ADR-012, section 1); a profile is one file and one line of the matching table, so adding a model is one PR.
- [ ] **16. Apps that draw their own title bars look like ours.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-09, who finds every toolkit's title bar different on Linux: what an app's own bar takes from the system is set once, from files and settings, never by repainting the app. shell-ui's settings portal serves `org.gnome.desktop.wm.preferences` `button-layout` from `layout.window_buttons` and the title bar button keys (M5.18a), so GTK and libadwaita apps put close, minimize and maximize where ours are and show the same ones; our close, minimize, maximize and restore glyphs from `design/icons/` are installed under the freedesktop symbolic names (`window-close-symbolic` and the rest) in an icon theme the base ships, so those apps draw our glyphs; an app that offers both kinds of bar gets ours (xdg-decoration and KDE's server decoration already prefer it, M4.4, M5.6a). An app's own header bar keeps its layout, tabs and buttons, and apps with fully custom bars keep them: forcing them would break them (Reliable over Beautiful). Beyond GNOME and macOS: one setting moves the buttons of our bars and of every GTK app's together, and the person's own icons in `design/icons/` reach both.
  Done when: `gdbus call` reads the portal's `button-layout` as `close,minimize,maximize:` with `layout.window_buttons = "left"` and as `:minimize,maximize,close` without it; a GTK 4 header bar window in desktop-test shows its close button at the left after the key is set, and its close glyph's pixels match our icon's. Notes: Flatpak apps see the host's icon themes (check in CI); nothing runs after start, as the portal answers only when asked.

**Parts after M6:** plus Flatpak, portals, portal-wlr, a store front, doas, fcitx5, at-spi2 (all reused). **Deliberately not added:** an ISO, a squashfs live system, a separate installer app, polkit unless already present, a store of our own, a portal backend process of our own.

**Principles check:** Reliable (the fresh install is tested the same way as updates; apps never block boot); Simple (the disk image is the live USB and the installer); Functional (a usable desktop offline, the basics as a CI artefact with an honest hardware column).

## M7 (next): Extend: developer mode, add-ons, fleets, servers, profiles

**Goal:** tinkerers turn on developer mode, change `/usr`, install packages with apk and add services, and reset it all with one command; add-ons extend the base as signed images that roll back with the slot; a fleet image carries a baked settings file; a server runs containers, comes up configured from a seed volume and updates unattended in a window; developers, gamers, office and creative users get a profile with a CI check each; game mode uses the hardware fully.

**Why here:** Powerful, Scalable and Versatile are principles 7 to 9, and nothing before the shell needed these. Each is a file or an add-on, not a part.

**Depends on:** M6.

- [ ] **1. Developer mode and reset.** `[system] developer_mode = true` (ADR-007 level 3; it was called unlocked mode until 2026-10-02) makes `edel boot mount-data` overlay `/usr` with the upper at `/data/developer/usr`, so OS files can be edited, `apk add` works against the overlay (the repositories file names Alpine's mirror for the image's branch) and custom OpenRC services can be added; `edel settings reset` deletes the upper after confirmation; a Settings switch behind a warning; `edel status` and the panel show "developer mode"; `edel settings export` reports how many files under `/usr` were changed but does not carry them (add-ons are the replicable way). First the images are built `/usr`-merged with Alpine's `merge-usr`, unless Alpine has merged by then: Alpine 3.24 keeps busybox in `/bin`, the C library in `/lib` and apk's database in `/lib/apk/db` (checked 2026-10-02), so a layer on `/usr` alone could not take `apk add`. The layer lives on `/data`, which both slots share, so when GRUB finds no slot with tries left it adds `edel.safe` to the command line and `edel boot mount-data` skips the developer layer and add-ons until the person resets or switches developer mode off; nothing destructive goes into the boot menu.
  Done when: turn it on, write `/usr/bin/hello`, `apk add` a small package, reboot, both still there; reset, gone; off, EROFS and apk fails; a developer-mode service that hangs makes both slots fail, and the next boot comes up with `edel.safe` and without the layer. Notes: same mechanism as the `/etc` overlay; off by default on phones; an update keeps the overlay, and reset is the documented fix when the overlay breaks one.
- [ ] **2a. Add-ons as signed per-release images.** A feature file with `addon = true` (M4.0; no separate `addons/` format); the add-on build moves the packages' `/etc/init.d/*` to `/usr/lib/edel/init.d/`, the rest of `/etc` to `/usr/lib/edel/etc/NAME/` and `/var` to `/usr/lib/edel/var/NAME/`, and `edel image build` refuses an add-on tree with files outside `/usr` after the move; `edel settings apply` links the `/etc` entries and init scripts into the `/etc` overlay and the runlevel at boot and removes its links when the feature is gone, so a rollback leaves no dangling service, and `edel boot mount-data`'s `/var` top-up (M1.3) copies the `/var` entries; CI copies each add-on's `summary` into its `release.toml` entry and builds each add-on with apk against the exact base (apk stays build-only, as the principles map says) into an `.ext4.gz` listed in `release.toml` with sha256 and the base version; `edel update` fetches and verifies the add-ons named in `[addons] add` with the same code as the slot, stores them on `/data/addons/<base-version>/`, and `edel boot mount-data` mounts them as extra lowerdirs of the one `/usr` overlay from step 1 (one mount with several lowers, not a layer per add-on); an add-on built for another base version is refused, so add-ons roll back with the slot; an add-on named in `[addons] add` that the new signed manifest does not list is skipped and reported (`add-on NAME is not in VERSION`) instead of keeping the slot off, and one the image already bakes is skipped as built in; these rules ship in this first add-on release, because the old slot's updater is the one that decides (ADR-008); `edel addon list` prints available and installed add-ons with their summaries and the line `edel settings set addons.installed+=NAME`; first real add-on `virtualization`. If a package ever needs patching, `packages/` and `ci/packages.sh` (abuild, a CI key, `edel image build --repository DIR`, public mirror lines only in the image's repositories file) start here.
  Done when: ab-test with a `ci-hello` add-on built from the CI feature file `ci/features/ci-hello.toml` and `ci/features/ci-hello/` (with `/etc/ci-hello.conf` and an init script, both linked on the new slot): with only 10.0.2.2 reachable, the new slot has `/usr/bin/ci-hello`, the rollback slot does not, the slot's sha256 still matches the manifest; a failing add-on fetch leaves the slot off and the running system untouched; a machine naming `ci-gone`, which the new manifest does not list, installs the update, confirms the slot and prints the notice; a cargo test for the `/etc` and `/var` move; cargo tests for the manifest and the lowerdir list. Notes: identical bits on every machine (ADR-007); USB and offline installs keep working; the Pages limit never touches a machine's update. A CI check fails when two add-ons, or an add-on and the base, ship the same path; Alpine's triggers write combined files (icon caches, compiled GSettings schemas) that cannot be stacked across layers, so this step decides how add-ons treat them (Duranium found this).
- [ ] **2b. An add-on chosen today arrives at the next boot.** `edel settings apply` (network phase) fetches the add-ons named in `[addons] add` that are missing for the running base version into `/data/addons/<base>/pending/`, with the code of step 2a, and sets a one-shot grubenv variable; at the next boot `edel boot mount-data` clears the variable, then mounts the pending add-ons with the active ones, and the guard's mark-good moves them to active; a boot that hangs or crashes resets, finds the variable cleared and pending add-ons present, comes up without them and records the failure in `last-fallback.toml`; a name removed from the list is not mounted at the next boot; `edel install` fetches a chosen profile's add-ons into the target's pending directory when online, so its first boot has them (ADR-008).
  Done when: on a confirmed slot, `edel settings set addons.installed+=ci-hello`, apply and a reboot give `/usr/bin/ci-hello` on the same slot; a `ci-hang` add-on whose init script blocks forever comes back without it and with the failure recorded; cargo tests for the pending, promote and discard transitions. Notes: reuses the grubenv code and the guard; no new part.
- [ ] **3. Fleet images.** `edel image build DEF --settings FILE [--settings OVERRIDE]` copies the merged file to `/usr/share/edel/settings.toml`; later files win on conflicts; `docs/FLEET.md`. Unattended install: when a seed's file holds an `[install]` table (`disk = "auto"`, the one disk M6.6a's `--list` rule picks: non-removable, not the boot medium, large enough; `erase = "empty-only"` by default, while `"yes"` needs a `/dev/disk/by-id/` name and a ten-second console countdown; `then = "poweroff"` by default, or `"reboot"`), the `edel-install` service (default runlevel, `command_background=yes` and a pidfile, like the guard) waits until `edel status` shows the running slot confirmed, then runs `edel install --unattended`, which reads `[install]` and implies `--yes`; after a success it writes `/data/edel/installed-to` (the by-id name and the date) on the stick and skips while that file names a disk present in the machine; a file that leaves nobody able to log in is refused; only `edel install` reads `[install]`, export never writes it and the copy on the target drops it (ADR-008).
  Done when: a seed with `[install] disk = "auto"` installs onto a blank vdb with no test service, serial shows the slot confirmed before the first write to vdb, and vdb then boots alone to the seed's hostname with no `[install]` in its file; booting the stick again after an `erase = "yes"` install prints `already installed to <id>` and vdb's sha256 is unchanged; a partitioned vdb is refused with `install refused: /dev/vdb is not empty` and its sha256 unchanged; CI builds two VMs from `ci/fleet/base.toml` plus two device files and both boot with their hostnames; cargo test for the merge.
- [ ] **4. Server image that runs containers.** (Since 2026-10-06 the server is the one image of M8.17 with the server's settings, not a definition of its own; until M8.17 lands, `images/server.toml`.) `images/server.toml` (vm's features plus `containers`, the same feature file desktops add as an add-on in step 6: podman, distrobox, fuse-overlayfs, passt, check; no getty on tty1); the seed is the `EDEL-SEED` volume from M2.2 or, in a cloud, a plain `settings.toml` given as the instance's user data, which the `cloud` feature fetches from the provider's metadata address at first boot (ADR-009, Alimardon's choice of 2026-10-06); `ci/server-test.sh` makes the seed with mkfs.vfat and mcopy; `edel image build` also emits qcow2.
  Done when: server-test sshes in with the seeded key, runs `edel status`, `podman load` of our own `edel-container-x86_64.tar.gz` (no network) and `podman run --rm edel:ci echo ok` as the seeded admin user, and `findmnt /var/lib/containers` shows `/data`. Notes: no cloud-init, tiny-cloud or cloud-config parsing: the user data is the settings file itself; each provider whose metadata address differs is one module of `features/cloud`; server-test serves user data from a local metadata stub (check in CI) and the VM takes its hostname from it.
- [ ] **5. Unattended updates.** `edel update` from the machine's channel (no release named, M5.8c), which also writes `/run/edel/reboot-needed`, run by busybox crond in the server image only; `[updates] auto` and `window` in `settings.toml`; servers and VMs reboot in the window; desktops only flag it through shell-ui's own timer (M5.9) and Settings shows it.
  Done when: a boot-test variant where the host serves a newer `release.toml` and the server VM has `auto = "boot"` ends with the other slot at ok=1 with no manual command. Notes: crond ships with busybox; it runs only where unattended reboots are the feature.
- [ ] **6. Profiles and dev containers, each with a CI check.** `profiles/{gaming,developer,creative,office}.toml` as system-file fragments merged by `[system] profiles`; a docs page for people coming from Ubuntu or RHEL maps their habits (`systemctl` to `rc-service`, `journalctl` to the logs, `apt` and `dnf` to Flatpak, containers and dev containers) (ADR-009); `features/containers.toml` with `addon = true` (the server image bakes the same file); developer = containers add-on plus an editor Flatpak; office = LibreOffice plus the `printing` add-on (`features/printing.toml`, `addon = true`, `switchable = true`); the installer page and the System page offer the profiles as one optional choice and write only `[system] profiles`, never a profile's contents, so a renewed profile reaches every machine that named it (ADR-008); creative = low-latency PipeWire quantum, screen capture, pen input; gaming = Steam, `steam-devices` udev rules, uinput group membership, game mode.
  Done when: cargo tests merge profiles deterministically; developer: `podman run --rm edel:ci echo ok` as the admin user and diff is empty; office: `lpstat -r` reports running and the printing row of basics-test turns PASS; creative: `pw-metadata` shows the quantum, the ScreenCast portal returns a stream, `wayland-info` lists `zwp_tablet_v2`; gaming: the Steam Flatpak (or a local stand-in built with flatpak-builder) installs and its `.desktop` appears in the launcher, the udev rules and group are present, `wayland-info` lists tearing-control. Notes: ADR-007 escape hatch 3; controllers, VA-API and pen pressure stay hardware rows. Seam (ADR-011): roles for old devices (M7.10m) are profiles too, so a profile must be able to start and stop the services a role needs.
- [ ] **7. Game mode.** VRR through DRM properties when the output supports it and a fullscreen game runs; tearing-control-v1; direct scanout for fullscreen surfaces; Super+G and the gaming profile; on only in game mode or on AC.
  Done when: cargo tests for the decision; a fullscreen test client logs `scanout=direct` or `scanout=unavailable (virtio)` without crashing. Notes: Efficient over Powerful on battery; real numbers from Alimardon.
- [ ] **8. Power behaviour without a daemon.** shell-ui applies `[power]` from M6.10: it reacts to UPower AC and battery events, sets `platform_profile` through `edel power` (sysfs), and does what `[power] lid` says on lid close, lock then suspend by default (lid via `testclient uinput`).
  Done when: unit tests for the decisions; the lid path passes in the VM, the rest is marked needs-hardware. Notes: CPU-level builds (`-march=x86-64-v3`) are a measurement, not a step: build only our own crates that way once, benchmark through `edel report` on the laptop, and only a visible gain reopens the question (principle 2's rule); a Mesa rebuild is off the plan until then.
- [ ] **9. AI agents, an optional add-on.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-06, after Omarchy built AI agents into its core: Edel OS offers them too, but only for people who choose them. `features/ai-agents.toml` with `addon = true` (M7.2a) brings the agent apps as Flatpaks or in a dev container (M7.6), never into the base; adding one, swapping one agent for another or dropping them all is that file and one PR, and on a machine one line, the add-ons key in the settings file. An agent acts as the person who runs it and changes the system only through the commands everyone uses (`edel settings set`, `edel update`), so each change shows in `edel settings diff` and is undone by `edel settings reset` or a rollback; it gets admin rights only when the person gives them, as any program does. Nothing runs in the background until a person starts an agent; we send nothing anywhere; an agent's account and key are the person's, kept by the agent, and the settings file only refers to them (ADR-006). Which agents the add-on carries is chosen in the step, by the principles, and named in a default row.
  Done when: CI adds the add-on to the desktop test image, a stand-in agent (a test script) sets a key with `edel settings set` and the change shows in `edel settings diff`; dropping the add-on leaves no file of it in the slot, no process and `diff` empty. Notes: no agent API, plugin or service of our own; an agent that needs one waits for a written reason (principle 3); a Settings row and a launcher entry come with the add-on's apps.
- [ ] **10. Your devices as one ([ADR-011](ADR-011-your-devices-as-one.md)).** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08 ("more seamless and interchangeble and more versatile", "far greater and better" than Googlebook, Apple, HarmonyOS NEXT and NVIDIA's DGX Spark, "as we are running same os everywhere"). Every device runs the same system, so devices join at the level of the system, for every app at once, paired without an account or a cloud. In thirteen parts, with M9.10, M9.11 and M13 for the parts that wait for the stable OS:
  - [ ] **10a. Phones of other systems.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07, after the review of HarmonyOS's "super device" and Apple's Continuity: an add-on `devices` (ADR-007 level 2) that pairs a phone with the computer over the open KDE Connect protocol, through a small GTK app that speaks it (Valent, as far as we know; chosen in the step), so Android phones with the KDE Connect app work from the first day and Edel OS phones (M9) speak it natively. Paired by a QR code Settings shows, over the local network only, encrypted, with no account and no cloud: notifications from the phone in the panel, replies to messages, a shared clipboard, files sent either way, the phone's battery in the panel, media control, finding the phone, and the phone as a touchpad and keyboard. Beyond Continuity and HarmonyOS: no vendor account and no single brand of phone; every paired device and what it may do is a line of the settings file, so a person's whole set of devices moves to a new computer with it; one switch per feature in Settings and `edel settings`; and the same pairing later docks the phone as a whole desktop (M9.9).
    Done when: CI pairs the add-on with a second VM running the same protocol's test peer, a notification sent from the peer shows in the panel, a file sent arrives in Downloads, and the clipboard crosses both ways; removing the add-on leaves no service running. Notes: no background service unless a device is paired (principle: run nothing the person is not using); the protocol is reused, not ours.
  - [ ] **10b. Pairing, one clipboard and every device's files.** Edel OS devices pair once with a code shown on one and scanned or typed on the other, no account; each is a `[devices.NAME]` table in the settings file with what it may do, so the set moves with the file and a fleet's file pairs its machines. The clipboard is one across paired devices, each device's files show in the others' file manager, and chosen folders stay in step peer to peer (a reused sync tool, chosen in the step). Beyond Googlebook and Continuity: no account or vendor, and the pairing travels in one file.
    Done when: two VMs in CI pair by code, a copied text crosses both ways, a file in one's chosen folder appears in the other's, and `edel settings export` lists the device; unpairing stops every service the pairing started.
  - [ ] **10c. Windows that move, and "Run on" a stronger machine.** "Move to" and "Open on" in the window's menu and the launcher show any app's window on another paired device while the app keeps running where it started, over the Wayland protocol through a reused forwarder (waypipe, as far as we know); "Run on" starts an app, a terminal or a dev container on a stronger paired machine with its window here, as NVIDIA Sync does for notebooks. Beyond Continue On, Handoff and HarmonyOS's continuation: every app moves, with no change to the app, and the strongest machine lends its power to any app, not one kind of work.
    Done when: in CI a test client started on one VM shows its window on the other, its colour checked in the second VM's screendump, and frames reach it within a latency budget in `ci/budgets.toml`; cutting the link leaves the app running on the first VM.
  - [ ] **10d. One pointer and keyboard across devices.** The compositor treats a paired device's screen as one more screen in `[displays]`, placed beside the others in Settings' Displays page: the pointer and keyboard cross onto it at the edge, as onto a second monitor. Beyond Universal Control: arranged like any screen, in one file, between any Edel OS devices.
    Done when: two VMs side by side in `[displays]`; `qmp.py move` past the first screen's edge moves the second VM's pointer, and typed keys reach a window there.
  - [ ] **10e. A device as another's screen or camera, and setup from another device.** A tablet or phone becomes an extra screen for a laptop, a phone's camera a webcam, and the welcome (M6.12) offers "Set up from my other device", copying the settings file, Wi-Fi networks and the apps' list encrypted end to end, secrets only by reference (ADR-006). Beyond Sidecar, Continuity Camera and Googlebook's setup transfer: any of your devices for any other, in either direction.
    Done when: a second VM shows the first's extra screen with a test window on it in its screendump, and a fresh VM set up from a paired one ends with the same `edel settings export`.
  - [ ] **10f. Your setup follows you.** Per section of the settings file, the person chooses what paired devices share (the look, shortcuts, apps, Wi-Fi networks, permissions); a change shows on the others live, the layout stays each device's own. "Move this desk to" carries a whole workspace's windows to another device (10c), and an app installed on one is offered on the others. Beyond Apple's and Google's sync: no account, every setting rather than a vendor's chosen few, and whole workspaces, not one task.
    Done when: two paired VMs; `edel settings set appearance.mode=dark` on one turns the other dark (its panel colour in the screendump), while `layout.preset` stays each one's own; a workspace of two test windows moves across.
  - [ ] **10g. One update for all your devices.** One paired device downloads a release and the others take it from it on the local network, checking the same signature; the first to confirm its new slot tells the others it is safe, and if it rolls back the others wait and say why. Beyond any vendor: a household's own devices are each other's early warning, and each release is downloaded once. Not delta updates and not a mirror network, both out of the plan.
    Done when: two paired VMs; the second updates from the first with the release server unreachable, its signature checked; a release that rolls back on the first leaves the second on its slot with `edel status` saying why.
  - [ ] **10h. Your devices back each other up.** `/data` (the settings file, `/etc`'s changes, the homes) copied encrypted to a paired device the person picks, on their schedule; the welcome (M6.12) restores a new or repaired device from it. Beyond Time Machine and cloud backups: no cloud, and any device can keep another's.
    Done when: a VM's marker files in `/home` and a changed setting come back on a fresh VM restored from its paired backup.
  - [ ] **10i. Every device's hardware is yours.** Speakers and microphones over PipeWire's network audio, printers and scanners, a phone's mobile data when the laptop has no Wi-Fi, and storage, lent between paired devices. Beyond Continuity: any brand, and every kind of hardware the system knows.
    Done when: a test tone played on one VM is captured on the other's PipeWire sink, and a test page reaches the other's print queue.
  - [ ] **10j. Find, lock, unlock, and one of everything.** Ring, locate (when shared), lock or wipe a lost device from another, through the person's own tunnel when away; a nearby phone unlocks the laptop, if turned on; a notification dismissed on one leaves all, one "do not disturb", one search across devices in the launcher, and every device's battery in quick settings. Beyond Find My and Smart Lock: no account, and search and notifications are one across every device.
    Done when: a lock sent from one VM locks the other (its lock screen in the screendump); a notification dismissed on one disappears from the other; a launcher search on one finds a file that exists only on the other.
  - [ ] **10k. Help me, with one button.** "Help me" in quick settings lets a paired helper see the screen and, if allowed, take over, only while it is on and with the panel showing it, over M6.3's screen sharing and 10c's window move. Beyond remote-help products: no account, built in, for every age.
    Done when: a second VM sees the first's screen with a test window in its screendump while help is on, and nothing once it is off.
  - [ ] **10l. Undo for your whole setup.** Every change to the settings file kept as a version on every device; Settings and `edel settings` show the history as changed lines and bring back any earlier state. Beyond restore points: one small text file, every device, readable.
    Done when: three `edel settings set` changes, then going back to the first state, `edel settings diff` empty against it.
  - [ ] **10m. A second life for every old device.** Roles (home server, backup keeper, second screen, webcam, speaker, photo frame, update source) are profiles (M7.6) chosen in Settings in one step. Beyond any vendor: old devices stay useful on the same system.
    Done when: a VM given the backup-keeper role in CI accepts another's backup (10h) with no other setting changed.
- [ ] **11. Hardware packs (ADR-012).** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-08, who wants laptops to work out of the box offline, with downloading as an option: firmware not on the always list (M5.30) becomes hardware packs, signed add-ons (ADR-007, level 2), one per firmware family, built with each release from Alpine's packages, each listing the hardware IDs (modalias, DMI) it serves. `edel` matches the machine's own IDs against the packs' lists, a pure function, with nothing sent anywhere; an installed pack lives under `/data`, where the kernel finds it through `firmware_class.path`, so the slots stay byte-identical (M1.12); an update brings the packs of its version and a rollback keeps those of the version it goes back to. A device without its pack is named by `edel status`, Settings and one notification, with one button or `edel addon add NAME` to install it; nothing is installed without the person's word. Beyond Windows Update's drivers and Ubuntu's additional drivers: matched offline from the stick first, the same signed packs on every machine of a fleet through the settings file, and no hardware ever reported to a server.
  Done when: CI builds a test pack with a release and signs it; install-test installs a VM whose PCI IDs match it and finds its file under `/data` and the kernel's firmware path pointing there; a cargo test matches sample modaliases to packs; `edel status` names the pack of a device that has none. Notes: the always list is M5.30's owner file; the stick carrying every pack and the online install fetching them are M8.17's.

**Parts after M7:** the part count is unchanged; add-ons and profiles are files; reused podman, distrobox, cups, Steam, a KDE Connect app (M7.10a), a Wayland forwarder and a folder sync tool (M7.10b, M7.10c). **Deliberately not added:** an AI agent in the base or running unasked, a gamemode daemon, TLP or power-profiles-daemon, a container tool of our own, cloud-init, apk at update time, a second updater, a second signing scheme in the runtime path.

**Principles check:** Reliable (add-ons are verified bits that roll back with the slot); Simple (add-ons and developer mode reuse the one overlay; apk at runtime only in developer mode); Scalable (the same file for one machine or a fleet, a server that runs containers); Powerful and Versatile served through files and switches, with Efficient winning on battery.

## M8 (next): First public release

**Goal:** a download page offers signed desktop, laptop, server and container images with platform level 1 published; the installer can encrypt `/data`; security rebuilds happen monthly without anyone asking; the base moves to the next Alpine branch with the upgrade proven from the previous one; the previous yearly release stays alive on its own branch; every release passes fresh install, upgrade from every kept release, rollback, hang, budgets and basics on every image; Alimardon's hardware checklist is a report, not a memory. Publishing the release itself is Alimardon's call.

**Depends on:** M7.

- [ ] **1. `edel report`, complete.** The M3 report grows the budgets (frame-time percentiles, idle memory, boot time) and the basics-checklist answers in `report.toml`; Alimardon runs it on each laptop and attaches it to an issue; `docs/hardware/*.toml` render into a table on the site.
  Done when: cargo tests; a sample report renders in the Pages build.
- [ ] **2. Encrypted data partition.** `edel install` makes LUKS2 on `edel-data` (cryptsetup) by default on laptop and desktop installs (Alimardon's choice of 2026-10-06, ADR-009; `--no-encrypt` leaves it plain), while servers and VMs, which start unattended, stay plain unless `--encrypt`; `edel boot mount-data` unlocks it with `cryptsetup open` prompting on the console (a greeter prompt later); the installer page gets the checkbox. The passphrase is typed with M6.6b's serial helper (`ci/serial.py`).
  Done when: install-test with `--encrypt` boots with the passphrase typed over the socket and `/data` is on `/dev/mapper`. Notes: TPM unlock comes with M11.2, the passphrase staying as the way in; only data is encrypted; slots are signed and public; mkinitfs's cryptsetup feature unlocks only a root, so it does not apply. Touch-only devices need an on-screen prompt instead (unl0kr, in postmarketOS's repository, M9).
- [ ] **3. Monthly security rebuilds.** A scheduled workflow rebuilds all images from current Alpine stable, runs the full suite, opens a PR with the package diff and the version bump; a merge tags a draft release; `docs/SECURITY.md`.
  Done when: a dry run opens the PR. Notes: Claude merges green PRs; a red rebuild is an incident.
- [ ] **4. Alpine branch bump.** `images/*.toml` move to v3.25 (the first of several branch crossings before M10); the PR shows the package diff from the record.
  Done when: upgrade-test from the v3.24 entries in `levels.toml` passes, install-test and ab-test pass on the new branch.
- [ ] **5. The previous release kept alive.** A `release/YYYY` branch with CI, the monthly rebuild workflow and the upgrade test running on it, so the second year of fixes ADR-003 promises (including our own community backports) has a place to land; `levels.toml` on main keeps pointing at it; CI on `release/*` refuses changes under `design/` and `presets/` unless the commit message starts with `look-fix:` (an accessibility or broken-shortcut fix, which still passes the contrast test), so a security rebuild never changes what people see (ADR-008).
  Done when: a dry run opens the rebuild PR on both branches; a test PR touching `design/` on a `release/*` branch fails, and the same change as a `look-fix:` commit passes. Notes: this is what M10 tests the promise against.

Ventoy starts a disk image kept as a file on its own partition; the laptop and desktop images gain a small initramfs step (the `ventoy` feature) that finds that file, maps it as a disk (device-mapper over the file's blocks, as Ventoy's own vdisk boot does) and starts slot A from it, with `edel-data` inside the file, so what a person changes is kept. On such a disk `edel update` and `edel rollback` work as on any stick, and `edel install` copies to an internal disk as from a stick of its own. `docs/TRY-IT.md` gains the steps.
  Done when: CI makes a Ventoy stick image with the released laptop image copied onto it as a file, boots it in QEMU to the live session, and a second boot finds a file the first one wrote in `/home`. Notes: from memory, Ventoy starts unknown `.img` files only through its vdisk plugin (a `.vtoy` file), which needs the image's own help to find its disk; check that in the step. Ventoy's partition is exFAT, so the image file must be stored in one piece; the step says how to copy it so. The A/B layout stays as it is (Reliable); only the initramfs learns where its disk is.
- [ ] **17. One image for every machine.** Alimardon's choice of 2026-10-06 ("merging server/VM/desktop into one unified image later a better choice too"), replacing four images with two: one disk image for servers, VMs, desktops and laptops, and the container image, which cannot be a disk; the phone (M9.7) stays apart only as long as its hardware needs it. The one image boots everywhere with the same packages and versions; what it becomes is settings, picked by the installer or the seed (a server: no desktop started, ssh on; a desktop: the desktop, ssh off), never a second download. `images/vm.toml` and `images/desktop.toml` become one definition; the VM's small `virt` kernel gives way to `lts`, and a VM with no screen starts no desktop service, as no background service runs unless its user uses it. The stick may also install online (Alimardon, 2026-10-07: "Maybe we can offer online installer as an option too"): `edel install` with a release list (the channel's, as `edel update` reads it) downloads and checks the newest signed slot instead of copying the stick's own, so an old stick installs today's release, and the installer page offers both, offline the default (Reliable: it works with no network). The stick carries every hardware pack of its release beside its slots, its live session loads the ones the machine matches, and the installer copies only those, or fetches them in the online install (ADR-012). A smaller online-only stick is built only if the measurements show it is worth a second image. The stick stays light: Alimardon asked that the installer be "lightweight as much as possible without too much things inside it" yet fully functional, so the step sets a stick size budget in `ci/budgets.toml` below today's desktop image, measures what the desktop adds against a server, and cuts before it merges (M10.7's measurements start here); one way to install, the same steps on any machine.
  Done when: CI builds one disk image that boot-test, system-test, install-test, ab-test, flatpak-test and every desktop-test case pass on, a seed with the server's settings boots it with no desktop process running and sshd on, the stick fits its budget, and `docs/TRY-IT.md` names one file. Notes: an older machine updates to it as to any release, as the slots carry no image name (check in the step); the ADR that records it amends CLAUDE.md's "one base, four images" decision.
