A design review of everything a person meets.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-08, to keep developing fast now and review at the end: before the first public release, with M8.11 and M8.15, a review of the whole design as one, not part by part: the UI first, the look and feel of every surface (the panel, dock, launcher, switcher, title bars, Settings and each page, the welcome, the installer) at every size class, and as much the words and shapes beneath it, the Settings page names, the settings file's sections and keys, the `edel` commands and their options, the feature and preset files, and every message (MESSAGES.md), so the GUI, the command line and the files read as one system, aligned, short and beautiful (ADR-008's same names decision). One Opus review agent reads it all and proposes changes, and Alimardon and the session walk the proposals together; it also looks for bugs and errors as it goes. Each accepted change is its own PR; a rename keeps the old name working through a format migration (M2.6), so nobody's settings file breaks. `docs/REVIEW-design.md` records what was found, what changed and what was left with its reason.
  Done when: `docs/REVIEW-design.md` lists every finding with its PR or its reason, every renamed key or command has a migration test, and the docs site builds with it. Notes: the M8 review Alimardon expects (CLAUDE.md's Reviews rule) is this one, with M8.11's bug reading; it changes names only where the review shows a real gain, as each rename costs a migration.
- [ ] **19. How releases are named, decided again.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-08 that the name people see be thought through again before the first public release, rather than settled now: whether a yearly base release is called by its year ("Edel OS 2027") with no codenames, by a number, or by a name, and how that name sits beside the build version (`YYYY.MM.N`, the "Hosting and versions" row), the monthly security images (ADR-003), the channels and the long channel (M11.5). The session writes the options with their consequences for people, support and the docs, Alimardon picks, and the choice becomes a row of "Decisions taken by default" with `os-release`'s `PRETTY_NAME` and the release notes (`TRY-IT.md`) following it.
  Done when: the row is in the table and a cargo test holds `PRETTY_NAME` in a built image's `os-release` to it. Notes: until then the image keeps its development name, `Edel OS 0.1 (development)` in `features/base`'s `os-release`, which no person has seen yet, so nothing has to be renamed later.

**Parts after M8:** the four parts plus cryptsetup (reused). **Deliberately not added:** a Secure Boot shim (users disable Secure Boot for the first release), an LTS channel, a bug tracker or forum of our own.

**Principles check:** Reliable and Functional gate the release; Efficient (reproducible bits, budgets); the platform level and the kept release branch start the compatibility clock for real.

## M9 (later): Phones, tablets, arm64

**Goal:** arm64 images build and pass the same tests as x86_64; the compositor and shell switch to Phone and Tablet form factors with touch, gestures, an on-screen keyboard and touch mode for apps; a phone image for one device family builds in CI, with hardware testing by Alimardon.

**Depends on:** M8.

- [ ] **1. Nightly arm64 VM.** `images/vm.toml` built for `aarch64` (under `arm64v8/alpine` with binfmt from `docker/setup-qemu-action`, or on an `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runner if the repository can use one); `ci/vm.sh` handles `qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt` with the `qemu-efi-aarch64` firmware and `console=ttyAMA0`; `boot.rs` already knows `arm64-efi`. Nightly on main, not per PR.
  Done when: the nightly arm64 boot-test reaches the login prompt within 15 minutes and ab-test passes with a longer timeout. Notes: moved here from phase 1; Rust under binfmt takes hours and keeping it green alone was not Reliable work.
- [ ] **2. arm64 full matrix.** All images for `aarch64`, desktop-test, install-test and ab-test under `qemu-system-aarch64`; nightly.
  Done when: the nightly matrix is green on both architectures.
- [ ] **3. Form-factor detection.** Output size and density, the libinput tablet-mode switch, touchscreen and keyboard presence decide Phone, Tablet or Desktop, with `[layout] device_type` as the override; Phone: one fullscreen window without a title bar; Tablet: side by side. When the form factor changes while running (a keyboard detached, a phone docked to a screen), the matching preset takes over on its own unless `layout.preset` is set, and the windows lay themselves out again; the exact flow is settled with Alimardon when M9 starts (asked on 2026-10-03).
  Done when: cargo tests for the classifier; desktop-test with `virtio-gpu-pci,xres=720,yres=1440` and virtio-tablet shows a fullscreen client; `testclient uinput` flipping SW_TABLET_MODE gives Tablet (QEMU's virtio-input cannot emit it).
- [ ] **4. Phone and Tablet presets, gestures, card switcher.** `presets/phone.toml` and `presets/tablet.toml`, both tiling by default (`[windows] policy = "tiling"`, as small touch screens suit it), which the panel's layout toggle or `layout.tiling` turns back to floating; shell-ui bottom gesture bar, swipe gestures, card-stack and card-grid switchers.
  Done when: screenshots per form factor are uploaded; a phone memory budget row exists.
- [ ] **5. On-screen keyboard.** squeekboard or wvkbd (check Alpine) through input-method-v2, started from the preset's session list in Phone and Tablet.
  Done when: tapping a text field in a test client raises the keyboard region and taps on keys type text.
- [ ] **6. Touch mode through the settings portal.** The touch-mode key named in `docs/layout-guide.md`, served by shell-ui's Settings interface from M5.5; libadwaita apps read it; it flips with the tablet-mode switch.
  Done when: a `gdbus` read returns the value and flips with the uinput switch in QEMU; Settings enlarges its rows.
- [ ] **7. Phone image for one device family.** `images/phone.toml` (aarch64) using postmarketOS device packages for a family that can boot our GPT and GRUB layout through U-Boot (SDM845, which Duranium already boots from a GPT inside `userdata` through U-Boot's UEFI, or whatever Alimardon chooses), ModemManager, developer mode off by default (ADR-007); CI builds it and boots pmOS's `qemu-aarch64` device variant.
  Done when: the image builds and the QEMU variant boots to the Phone preset; the real device is reported by Alimardon with `edel report`. Notes: first prove in QEMU that GRUB for arm64 started by U-Boot's UEFI writes `grubenv`, and write a second counter only if it cannot; `Layout` gains a sector size (4096 on UFS phones); calls, SMS and Waydroid are hardware-only follow-ups.
- [ ] **8. Rotation and 2-in-1 posture.** iio-sensor-proxy, `transform` in `[displays]`, a rotation lock in Settings.
  Done when: `transform = 90` moves the panel to the new top edge in the screendump; the sensor path is a needs-hardware row in the basics table (no accelerometer source in QEMU).
- [ ] **9. The phone as a whole desktop when docked.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07: a phone running Edel OS, plugged into a screen, keyboard and mouse (a USB-C dock with DisplayPort, or a wireless display later), becomes a full desktop with the person's preset on the big screen, running the same apps, as Samsung's DeX does, so one device can do everything. The form-factor switch of step 3 already moves to Desktop when a screen is docked; this step makes it whole: the windows move to the big screen and lay themselves out again, keeping their state (ADR-004's live-resize rule); the phone's own screen goes dark or becomes a touchpad, chosen in Settings; unplugging brings the Phone preset back with every window still open; the effect tier and the memory budget are measured on the phone docked.
  Done when: in QEMU, a second screen added to the phone image while it runs moves the windows to it under the Classic preset and removing it brings Phone back with the same windows; the real device docked is reported by Alimardon with `edel report`. Notes: level 1, settings (ADR-007), no new part: the compositor's screens (M4.6c) and step 3's switch do it; which phones carry video over USB-C is checked in step 7's device family.
- [ ] **10. Your desktop in your pocket ([ADR-011](ADR-011-your-devices-as-one.md), after the stable OS).** The phone carries the person's settings file and keys; a tap at any Edel OS computer logs them in as themselves in a guest session wiped when they leave, their apps and files served from the phone or their home machine. Beyond roaming profiles: no company server, any Edel OS machine.
  Done when: a VM with no account logs in as the phone VM's person, shows their look in the screendump, and keeps nothing after logout.
- [ ] **11. Your phone is your key (after the stable OS).** The Edel OS phone as passkey and security key (FIDO2) for the person's devices and websites, and as the laptop's unlock when near. Beyond Apple's and Google's passkeys: no vendor account.
  Done when: a test web login on the laptop VM completes with the phone VM as its authenticator.

**Parts after M9:** unchanged; squeekboard or wvkbd and postmarketOS device packages reused. **Deliberately not added:** a second shell for phones, a separate phone image pipeline, Waydroid until asked.

**Principles check:** Scalable (the same images and update path serve phones); Versatile through presets; Reliable (phones keep A/B and ship with developer mode off).

## M10 (later): The compatibility promise in practice

**Goal:** today's app platform installs on the oldest kept release as an add-on; a runtime archive and driver rebuilds for old runtimes exist; x86_64 apps run on arm64; the store shows device badges; Secure Boot is revisited.

**Depends on:** M8.

- [ ] **1. App platform as an add-on on the oldest kept release.** `features/platform.toml` (`addon = true`) built against the oldest entry in `ci/levels.toml` and applied through the same `edel update` path.
  Done when: CI installs the newest platform add-on on that release's VM image (from its kept assets) and runs a Flatpak app. Notes: ADR-005 section 1 without a second updater; M3.4 and M8.5 are what make an "oldest release" exist.
- [ ] **2. GL driver extension for old runtimes.** `org.freedesktop.Platform.GL.edel` for an end-of-life runtime built from current Mesa with flatpak-builder on the runner and published to our Flatpak repo.
  Done when: `glxinfo` inside the old runtime reports the new Mesa version. Notes: host injection stays the alternative (ADR-003 section 5).
- [ ] **3. Runtime archive.** An OSTree mirror of every runtime we have shipped, on release storage, as a lower-priority remote in images.
  Done when: `flatpak install` from the archive works in CI for one archived runtime with Flathub unreachable.
- [ ] **4. x86_64 apps on arm64.** Add-on `x86-emulation` (FEX-Emu or box64: check Alpine, else build in our repo).
  Done when: an x86_64 hello binary runs in the arm64 VM (smoke only, long timeout).
- [ ] **5. AppStream device badges.** The store front shows Phone, Tablet and Desktop from AppStream metadata, as an upstream contribution or a wrapper.
  Done when: a test app's metainfo renders with badges in a screendump.
- [ ] **6. Secure Boot with our own key.** Signed GRUB and kernels, `edel install --secure-boot` enrolling our certificate (no shim at first). The long channel this step also held moved to M11.5 on 2026-10-06 (ADR-009).
  Done when: OVMF with Secure Boot and our key enrolled boots the signed image and refuses an unsigned kernel.
- [ ] **7. Lighter than the lightest.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-06: Edel OS should be at least as small as Alpine where it can be, and lighter than other light distributions, "but still be much more powerful", with every feature, every convenience and the quality kept. Measure each image (the download, the installed slot, memory at idle and after login, CPU at idle, boot time) against Alpine's own images and other light distributions of the same kind, in a CI table; then cut what the measurements show, one PR each and never a feature: packages and files no feature needs, debug data, firmware no listed device uses, the initramfs, services that run without being used, the desktop's memory and wake-ups, the installer stick's size. Done when: the table runs in CI on every release with each image's numbers beside Alpine's and the others', and every budget in `ci/budgets.toml` has been lowered at least once from what it measured. Notes: really later, after the first public release; Instant, Efficient and Reliable decide each cut, and a cut that costs a basic from the basics checklist (M6.7) is not taken.

- [ ] **8. Our own toolkit for our apps, if it measures better.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-06, who wants our own apps "really clear" and beautiful, smooth and light, written in Rust, modular and easy to change, add to and upgrade. shell-ui already draws itself (smithay-client-toolkit, tiny-skia, cosmic-text, AccessKit); this step grows that code into one crate the shell and our own apps share, Settings first, with the widgets Settings uses (rows, switches, lists, cards, text fields with input methods, scrolling, focus) drawn from the same tokens and icons, and swaps it in for GTK4 in Settings only when CI measures it lighter and at least as smooth, with keyboard, screen reader and input methods all working. Settings keeps every toolkit call in `crates/settings/src/widgets.rs` and the files beside it (M5.6a), so the swap is one module. Done when: Settings on the crate passes desktop-test's `settings` case and the basics checklist (M6.7) with less memory than its GTK4 build's `settings_own_mib` and no frame over budget. Notes: the crate would be a fifth part, so it needs its written reason then (principle 3) and Alimardon's word before the swap; apps from others keep GTK, Qt or what they bring; libcosmic and iced are studied, not adopted (Alimardon, 2026-10-06).
- [ ] **9. Spike: an app that carries everything but the frozen part.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07: install "this application itself" and run it on any Edel OS version, with no runtime step, as Windows and macOS do ([the review](REVIEW-apps-and-lessons.md)). Three apps built from Alpine's packages as self-contained bundles (a GTK app, an app that draws with the GPU, and a command-line tool): each carries its own libraries and uses only the frozen part, the kernel, musl, the system's graphics drivers and the standard protocols (Wayland, PipeWire, D-Bus and the portals), sandboxed as Flatpak sandboxes. `ci/bundle-spike/` builds them and runs each on two bases a year apart, and measures size on disk, memory and start time against the same apps from Flathub.
  Done when: CI runs all three bundles on both bases, the GPU app drawing with the newer base's drivers, and writes the measurements into the run summary beside Flathub's. Notes: a spike, not a format: nothing ships from it, and using the base's musl and drivers would loosen "apps never link against the base" (ADR-003, ADR-005), which waits for Alimardon's words and an ADR if the numbers clearly win; glibc-only apps stay on Flathub either way.
- [ ] **10. Spike (optional): a Flatpak runtime built from Alpine's packages.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07, beside M10.9: one platform level's runtime built from that year's Alpine packages (musl), with three apps from Alpine on top, against the same apps on Flathub's runtime. Taken only if M10.9 does not settle the question.
  Done when: CI installs the runtime and the three apps from a local repository, runs them, and writes their size, memory and start time beside Flathub's. Notes: its graphics drivers come as a GL extension built for it, as M10.2 builds for old runtimes; an archive of old runtimes is M10.3.
**Parts after M10:** unchanged, unless M10.8's toolkit measures better and Alimardon says to swap it in. **Deliberately not added:** a second updater for the app platform, a Nix-style store, a shim, an erofs slot format until a measurement asks for it.

**Principles check:** Reliable and Scalable over the long run (the promise is checked against real old images that were kept on purpose); Simple is protected by routing the platform through the one update path.

## M11 (later): Enterprise appliances and the long channel

**Goal:** container hosts, edge devices and managed workstations that verify their own system at every boot, unlock their data with the TPM, keep an audit trail and pass published hardening checks, all as features and add-ons on the one base, with each protection that suits a laptop reaching every laptop too; and, once Alimardon says someone can carry it, a channel with ten years of support (ADR-009).

**Why later:** the consumer desktop comes first (Alimardon, 2026-10-06). The doors this milestone needs were opened earlier (M1.11, M1.12, M3.8, M7.4, M8.12), so nothing here changes a format people already have.

**Depends on:** M8; the boot protections also on M10.6 (Secure Boot).

- [ ] **1. A verified root.** dm-verity over each slot (byte-identical since M1.12), its root hash in the signed `release.toml` and on the kernel command line, checked by the initramfs; a slot that fails verification never starts, and GRUB falls back.
  Done when: ab-test flips one byte in slot B, and the machine refuses it and comes back on slot A.
- [ ] **2. TPM unlock of the data.** On machines with TPM 2.0, `/data` unlocks through the measured boot of M10.6; the passphrase from M8.2 stays as the way in.
  Done when: QEMU with swtpm boots without typing, and a changed loader asks for the passphrase.
- [ ] **3. Audit and access control.** The Linux audit framework as a feature for server and managed images; AppArmor profiles for our own services if Alpine's AppArmor measures well (check); never SELinux, which Alpine does not ship.
  Done when: an audit test finds a login and an `edel settings set` in the audit log.
- [ ] **4. Hardening checks in CI.** A CIS-style list of checks (a read-only system, ssh settings, open ports, loaded kernel modules) runs against every image, and each release publishes the results; nothing runs on machines.
  Done when: the checks run on every PR, and a test image with `PermitRootLogin yes` fails one.
- [ ] **5. The long channel, ten years as the goal.** A channel fed by one yearly release branch (M8.5) kept alive with our own backports for the kernel and every base package, its length written in `docs/RELEASE.md`. Starts only on Alimardon's word, when there are people or a sponsor to carry it (ADR-009).
  Done when: the channel's manifest is published, and a machine on it updates only within its branch.
- [ ] **6. A systemd adapter add-on, if asked.** An add-on (ADR-007) with `systemctl`-style start, stop, status and enable over OpenRC and `edel`, reading simple unit files, for people and tools coming from Ubuntu or RHEL; never in the base. Starts on Alimardon's word, when a real user needs it (ADR-009).
  Done when: an add-on test enables and starts a service from a simple unit file and `systemctl status` shows it running.
- [ ] **7. A drift check without an agent.** Server and managed images run `edel settings diff` daily from crond and write what drifted to the system log and `/run/edel/drift`; nothing is sent anywhere.
  Done when: server-test changes a key by hand, and the next run reports exactly that key.
- [ ] **8. Edge images with smaller slots.** The slot size becomes a choice of the image definition (today every install takes 4 GiB slots, M3.3b), so a device with 4 to 8 GB of flash keeps room for its data; a disk-layout change recorded in `docs/FORMATS.md`.
  Done when: an edge test image installs with 1 GiB slots and passes ab-test.
- [ ] **9. Review ZFS for data disks.** Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-08, after hearing OpenZFS called better: look again at OpenZFS when this milestone comes up, decide then, and build nothing before. The system disk stays as it is: two whole slots already give atomic updates and rollback (ADR-006), a snapshot-based root was rejected (ADR-007, option B), ZFS's license keeps it out of the kernel, so its module must be rebuilt for every kernel and a failed rebuild could stop a boot (Reliable), and its cache wants memory our budgets do not have (Efficient). What it could give is for people's data on servers and home NAS boxes: checksums against silent corruption, snapshots, compression, many disks as one, and copying a disk to another machine. The review is `docs/REVIEW-zfs.md`: what OpenZFS on Alpine and musl costs then (module rebuilds per kernel, memory, the license), what btrfs and plain ext4 with mdraid give for the same needs, and a verdict, for Alimardon to decide: nothing, or ZFS as an add-on (ADR-007, level 2) for data disks only, never the root.
  Done when: `docs/REVIEW-zfs.md` with its verdict is in `SUMMARY.md` and the docs site builds in Rust checks (`sh ci/site.sh build`).

**Parts after M11:** unchanged; audit, AppArmor, the firewall and the adapter are features or add-ons. **Deliberately not added:** an enterprise edition, an enterprise-only security tier, a management server or fleet agent, SELinux, a compliance daemon, certifications before there is a company.

**Principles check:** Reliable and Scalable drive it: a slot that verifies itself and health checks of the image's own choosing keep unattended machines safe. Simple holds, because everything here is a feature, an add-on or a CI check on the same base. Efficient: consumer images get only what protects them too (the verified root, TPM unlock). Traded off: a slower road to buyers who want a Red Hat style system.

## M12 (an idea, not scheduled): Glasses and pocket PCs

**Goal:** a pocket-sized PC runs the desktop image and shows it in display glasses worn instead of a monitor: the whole desktop with its apps, floating in front of the eyes, as Apple's Vision Pro shows a Mac's screen, with the full power of a PC rather than a phone's. Asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-07, after an announcement of Meta's glasses that work with a small device kept in a pocket (from Alimardon's words; the product's details are not checked here), as the newest and furthest idea, written down so it is not lost.

**Why an idea only:** it needs phones and small devices first (M9) and hardware Alimardon chooses; it starts only on their word, and each step below is planned in detail then.

**Depends on:** M9 (form factors, arm64, docking).

- [ ] **1. Glasses as a screen.** Most display glasses today plug into USB-C with DisplayPort and appear as an ordinary monitor, so the desktop already lights them (M4.6c); the step tries real glasses with Alimardon, finds the right scale, and adds a **Glasses** preset: larger text, the panel kept near the middle of the view, dark by default, as glasses' displays add light to what is behind them.
- [ ] **2. Screens fixed in space.** The compositor places the desktop on one or more virtual screens that stay still as the head turns, reading the glasses' motion sensor and drawing each frame for where the head is now; reused from Monado, the open OpenXR runtime, rather than written, if it measures well. It must keep Instant: a gate on the time from a head turn to the frame that shows it.
- [ ] **3. A pocket PC image.** A small fanless x86_64 or arm64 box, or a handheld, that Alimardon picks as the reference, with battery and heat budgets in `ci/budgets.toml`, and the phone docked (M9.9) as a second way in.
- [ ] **4. Glasses that link without a cable.** Only for glasses that speak an open protocol to a nearby device; checked when the step starts.

**Parts after M12:** unchanged if Monado is reused; step 2 is a module of the compositor behind its screens. **Deliberately not added:** a 3D desktop of our own, a store for spatial apps, any closed vendor runtime in the base.

**Principles check:** Scalable and Versatile drive it: one base from a container to a pocket PC, one more form factor through a preset. Simple holds: glasses are a screen first, so step 1 needs no new part. Instant gates step 2, and Reliable keeps every device on the same A/B updates. Traded off: it waits behind everything else, so it costs nothing now.

## M13 (later, after the stable OS): Devices as one, the deep parts

**Goal:** the two ideas of ADR-011 nobody has made work for everyday apps, measured before anything is promised.

**Depends on:** M8 (the stable OS) and M7.10.

- [ ] **1. Spike: apps that travel.** A running app frozen with its unsaved work on one device and resumed on another with CRIU, its window reconnected (ADR-011, section 17), tried on three apps.
  Done when: CI freezes a test client on one VM and resumes it on another, its window and its state (a counter it draws) intact, or the spike writes down why not.
- [ ] **2. Spike: all your devices as one computer.** Idle plugged-in devices helping with a big job (a build, a video export, a local AI model), only with consent and never on battery (section 18).
  Done when: CI runs one job split over two VMs and measures the time against one VM, with the result in the run summary.

**Principles check:** Reliable and Simple keep both as spikes until a measurement shows a gain people can see; the seams of ADR-011's section 19, kept now, are what let them come later without a rewrite.

## Decisions taken by default

Alimardon can overrule any of these by commenting; Claude then updates the plan and the log.

| Question | Default | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Keep writing the GRUB try counter every boot? | Yes, RAUC's scheme | Reliable over Efficient: a slot that starts hanging later must still fall back |
| Base before pixels? | M1 to M3 (about 19 PRs) land before the first compositor PR | Reliable first; the apk repo, a migration registry, a lock gate and arm64 were taken out of phase 1 because none of them is Reliable work; Alimardon can pull M4 steps 1 to 4 ahead of M2 and M3 if they want something to look at sooner |
| How is `edel` shipped in images? | `edel image build` copies the binary built inside `alpine:3.24` | Nothing needs apk resolution until a patched package or M7; `packages/` starts then; ed25519 is the only key scheme until it does |
| Persistent state | One `edel-data` partition: `/home` and `/var` bind-mounted (`/var` topped up from the slot every boot), `/etc` an overlay with its upper on `/data`, root read-only, mounted by a sysinit service | Zero new code beyond mounts; the upper directory is the "what did this machine change" list |
| Image facts | `EDEL_*` keys in `/usr/lib/os-release` (version, channel, level, health) | One standard file instead of a new format |
| Hang detection | `edel boot guard`, a background process started first in sysinit, pets softdog from the initramfs (linux-lts; linux-virt has none) and takes the hardware watchdog as soon as hwdrivers loads one (i6300esb in QEMU, iTCO_wdt or sp5100_tco on laptops) until the sentinel files exist, then exits; only a hardware watchdog restarts a frozen kernel | One mechanism for kernel hangs and a desktop that never starts; no daemon; softlevel is never trusted |
| Health on a desktop | `/run/edel/session/ready`, written by whichever compositor greetd starts, the greeter's included | "The login screen is up", so a machine waiting at the greeter is healthy |
| Slot size | 4096 MiB for every installed machine and the VM image from the first preview; the laptop stick keeps 1024 MiB slots to fit an 8 GB stick, and `edel install` copies its slot into a 4096 MiB one; update images are shrunk, and a slot keeps them so, byte for byte (M1.12) | A preview machine must take every later update |
| Signing | ed25519 manifests, two public keys in every image, downgrade refused; add-ons signed the same way | Rotation possible from day one; one scheme in the runtime path |
| Transport and compression | ureq with rustls, gzip; full images, no deltas | Static on musl; deltas only after a measurement |
| Bootloader updates | GRUB ships inside the slot; installed to the ESP at mark-good after the slot is confirmed; the loader is the one piece outside the A/B rollback, so every transition is exercised in CI | UEFI only; rare, reviewed changes |
| Installer and live media | The laptop or desktop disk image on a USB stick; `edel install` copies the running slot; the UI is a Settings page | One image, one pipeline, no ISO |
| Privilege for Settings | doas: admins change settings without a password, and type their own before installs, updates, rollbacks and root's ssh keys (M6.5); polkit only if NetworkManager drags it in | Reuse what is present; Reliable over convenience for what can erase a disk or change the system (R6 of the 2026-10-06 review) |
| Session and seat | seatd and greetd (under supervise-daemon), pam_rundir, no elogind; greetd owns the compositor's lifecycle; the compositor spawns shell-ui and the user services | Fewest parts, one supervisor per level; elogind only with a written reason |
| Shell, outputs and update settings | `[layout]`, `[displays]`, `[updates]` in `/data/edel/settings.toml`, optionally overridden by `~/.config/edel/settings.toml` with the same schema; watched with inotify; `state.toml` under `/run/edel/session`; one private protocol `edel-shell-v1` | One source of truth, one file per machine, no IPC daemon |
| Title bars | 28 px, the focused one lighter, a 1 px border in the bar's colour; maximize and close at the right; the title centred in Inter at 13 px, loaded on a thread so the first window never waits; 6 px grips outside the frame resize, 16 px from a corner resize from the corner; a double click on the bar maximizes; a maximized window keeps its bar and border and lets go once dragged 8 px (M4.4) | Cinnamon-like and one look for every window; the sizes and colours are tokens, so M5's presets and Appearance page change them without code |
| Window animations | Full: open 200 ms growing from 90 percent, close 150 ms, slide 200 ms; Balanced: 150 ms from 95 percent, 112 ms, 150 ms; Lite: fades only, 100 ms and 75 ms; ease-out cubic; reduced motion keeps the fades, off keeps nothing (M5.11b) | Instant and Beautiful: short enough never to make anyone wait, long enough to show where a window came from and went; one table in `animation.rs` |
| Presets | Built into `edel`'s library from `presets/NAME.toml`, one file each, read strictly by the tests and never written on a machine; a key joins the format with the step that first reads it; an unknown `layout.preset` is Classic, with a note (M5.1c) | Simple and Reliable: one copy that every part reads with one function, and no file on the machine that can go missing or drift from the release |
| Workspaces | A fixed number from the preset, Classic four, spanning every screen; each keeps its own policy and stacking order; Super+1 to 9 show one and Super+Shift+1 to 9 send the focused window there, on top; a hidden workspace's windows get no frame callbacks; a preset with fewer moves the windows of those that go to the last that stays (M5.2a) | Simple and Efficient: Cinnamon's model, with no workspace made or removed behind a person's back, and nothing drawn for what is not shown; a dynamic model waits for a preset that asks for it |
| Default look | [docs/mockups](mockups/README.md)' second round: corners a little rounded, never pill-shaped (windows 10 px, menus 12, controls 7, square on Lite), soft layered shadows from one light, one calm blue accent, Inter, muted app-icon colours, light and dark both first-class; every desktop preset's panel shows, towards its end, the round workspace buttons (at most three at once, the shown one and its neighbours, the rest a scroll away) and one layout button whose icon changes and which fills when tiled (M5.2c, M5.3, M5.5); title bars stay 28 px; app icons are drawn as objects, not glyphs on tiles | Beautiful and Versatile: a calm, polished default that developers, office workers, gamers, server admins and people of every age can live with, and every value a token anyone can change (M5.12); asked by Alimardon on 2026-10-03 |
| Shortcuts | Close Super+Q, launcher Super, switcher Alt+Tab, tiling Super+T, terminal Ctrl+Alt+T (foot until `default_apps.terminal`, M6.10), screenshot Print, lock Super+L, workspace N Super+N; close, launcher and lock are ways out that always keep keys; keys match where a Latin layout has them (M5.13a) | Functional and Versatile: the Classic preset's keys, close to Cinnamon's and GNOME's, until the presets ship their own (M5.4); one table in `shortcuts.rs` |
| Effect tiers | Lite on a software renderer, at most Balanced on battery, else Full; a frame misses when it takes over three quarters of the screen's refresh interval to draw, and 5 misses among the last 60 frames drop one tier, never raised again within the session (M5.11a) | Instant first: a slow machine sheds effects within a second of struggling; the thresholds are a guess until `edel report` from Alimardon's laptop, and live in one place (`effects.rs`) |
| XWayland | xwayland-satellite, started by the compositor when the first X11 app connects to the display it holds, and left running until the session ends; only in images with the `xwayland` feature; X11 windows get our bar unless they draw their own (M4.7a) | Nothing runs and no memory goes to X11 on a desktop without X11 apps (Efficient: about 45 MiB otherwise); an X11 crash stays out of the compositor (Reliable); no X11 window manager in our code (Simple). Traded off: one more process hop for X11 apps' frames, which no measurement has shown to matter |
| Sound | PipeWire with WirePlumber, PipeWire serving the PulseAudio socket itself rather than as a second process, `pipewire-pulse` (one process less, measured against the desktop memory budget), started per person by the compositor from the preset's `[session] start` (the release's list unless a preset names its own), never as services of the machine; the sound devices belong to group `seat`; the volume and the chosen device are WirePlumber's own state, so no settings file key holds them (M5.7b) | Efficient and Reliable: nothing runs on a machine nobody uses, and one copy of each value cannot disagree with itself; Simple: the stock PipeWire stack with no patches, which ADR-011's lent speakers need, and the one `wpctl` command for Settings, the panel and the command line. Traded off: PipeWire, WirePlumber and the PulseAudio server cost resident memory the desktop budget was set without (CI's step summary prints it), and the session list is read once at login, so a preset change takes effect at the next one |
| Network | NetworkManager on the desktop with its own DHCP client, replacing busybox `networking`, which the `vm` feature keeps for VMs and servers so exactly one thing manages eth0; wpa_supplicant (not iwd) for Wi-Fi, started by NetworkManager over D-Bus when an adapter appears and never otherwise; `loopback` gives `lo` its address; `wireless-regdb` gives the kernel each country's channels; no polkit: `auth-polkit=false` and a D-Bus policy that lets only root and group `seat` change anything, proven by `desktop-test network` (ci may, nobody may not); `libelogind` comes with NetworkManager but no elogind daemon runs; the connections and their passwords are NetworkManager's own state, never in the settings file, so the page has no Reset and `edel settings get network` shows only the device's name (M5.8a) | Reliable and Simple: the reused daemon that every panel, app and `nmcli` already speaks to, wpa_supplicant being the backend NetworkManager is tested with (iwd is smaller, and a swap is one package and one line of `90-edel.conf` if a measurement asks), and one policy file instead of polkit and a session tracker; Efficient: nothing for Wi-Fi runs on a machine without an adapter. Traded off: NetworkManager's resident memory, which CI prints (`network memory`) and the desktop budget must hold, and a Wi-Fi password is briefly in `nmcli`'s arguments, visible to the people at the machine, until `nmcli --ask` or a secret agent replaces it |
| Bluetooth | BlueZ's bluetoothd started with the machine, idle without an adapter (CI prints its memory), not started on demand: a udev rule that starts a service during boot can stall it; a D-Bus policy for root and group `seat` replaces BlueZ's, which lets every local user pair; the Settings page pairs with `bluetoothctl --agent NoInputNoOutput` ("Just Works": headphones, speakers, mice and most phones; a keyboard that shows a code to type waits for a passcode answer, a seam in `crates/settings/src/bluetooth.rs`), trusts and connects; WirePlumber's Bluetooth monitor is `optional` (not the stock `required`) so a plugin that fails to load never stops sound, turned on by the `bluetooth` feature's `91-edel-bluetooth.conf` after `sound`'s `90-edel.conf` turned it off for images without Bluetooth (M5.8a) | Simple and Reliable: the stock daemon started the stock way, and sound survives a Bluetooth fault; Efficient traded off for Simple: a daemon runs on machines with no adapter until a measurement shows a cost people can see |
| Tiling algorithm | Master-stack first: the first window takes the left half, the rest share the right half from the top, 8 px apart and from the edges (the `gap` token); a new window joins the bottom of the stack, and when the master closes the first of the stack takes its place (M4.5) | Simplest to implement and test; the window being worked in stays put; `split` and `scroll` join behind the same trait as styles (M5.16) |
| Settings' renderer | GTK's Cairo renderer, unless `GSK_RENDERER` names another; `RENDERER` in `crates/settings/src/main.rs` | Efficient: in CI's VM Settings held 18.6 MiB of its own with Cairo against 107.4 MiB with GL under llvmpipe (12.8 against 88.3 MiB in a nested session), and a page of rows needs no GPU to keep up; undone by one line if a measurement on hardware shows GL is needed (2026-10-07, PR #113) |
| Tiling styles | `stack` in every preset; one style for the person, set by `layout.tiling_style`, while Super+T stays per workspace; choosing a style never switches a workspace between floating and tiling, so a floating one takes the style when it next tiles (Alimardon chose this on 2026-10-04, over tiling it at once); with `scroll`, a new column is half the screen wide (M5.16) | Simple: one key that the menu, Settings and `edel settings set` share, and each control does one thing, Super+T and the button's click the switch, the menu the style; Versatile: a preset names its own style in one line |
| Graphics in CI | virtio-vga with llvmpipe (`LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`, `LP_NUM_THREADS=0`, M4.1), GLES only, QMP screendump, pixel-region checks, a Rust test client (also `sni` and `uinput` subcommands); no golden images | Robust to fonts and antialiasing; one render path |
| Reference hardware and budgets | Alimardon's 2016 integrated-graphics laptop (they name the model); the CI gate is llvmpipe frame p99 and idle frames against the merged baseline, first set from sway; real frame numbers through `edel report`; boot and memory as in-guest medians of three | Principle 2's measurement cannot run in CI, so this is the stated substitute |
| Kernel | linux-lts in every bootable image | One module list, one initramfs, ADR-003's same packages everywhere; virt only with a measured reason |
| Package record | The installed list inside the image and in the release; diffs shown, never gated | A true lock needs a mirror; the monthly rebuild is the bump |
| Add-ons | Signed per-release images, extra lowerdirs of the one `/usr` overlay, refused on another base version | Identical bits, offline installs, apk stays in CI |
| Flatpak installs | Per user (`--user`); the default app set is a read-only system installation inside the slot | No polkit; data in `/home`; a usable desktop offline |
| Developer mode (was unlocked mode) | An overlay upper on `/usr` stored on `/data`, apk allowed inside it, reset deletes it | Same mechanism as `/etc`; the one place where OS bits may change |
| What a user may customize | Three levels (ADR-007): settings in `settings.toml` for everyone (look, layout, shortcuts, default apps, startup, power, optional services), signed add-ons and profiles, developer mode for OS bits | Anything that is a value in the settings file is a setting; anything that changes OS bits is developer mode |
| Cloud and server provisioning | One seed label `EDEL-SEED` holding `settings.toml`; search order live file, seed, ESP, baked | One way to describe a machine, no cloud-config |
| Reused components to check in CI | foot, eudev, pam-rundir, growpart, Bazaar, xdg-desktop-portal-wlr with grim, fcitx5, squeekboard or wvkbd, Inter and Noto fonts, Adwaita icons and cursor | Smallest existing piece that fits; swap if missing on Alpine 3.24 |
| Hosting and versions | GitHub Releases for images (kept forever), Pages for manifests; versions `YYYY.MM.N`; channel `stable`; a rolling `preview` pre-release | No infrastructure of ours; old releases exist to test the promise against |
| arm64 in CI | M9, nightly, on arm runners if the repository can use them, else binfmt | TCG is slow; keep it off the per-PR path and out of phase 1 |
| Encryption and Secure Boot | LUKS2 on `/data` unlocked by `edel boot mount-data`, an installer option in M8; no shim for the first release | One reused package; shim signing is a process we cannot run from CI |
| App platform independence (ADR-005) | A `platform` add-on through the same slot update, from M10, against the oldest entry in `levels.toml` | One update path; the level is a contract, not a mechanism |
| Slot filesystem | ext4 | The slot format is the most expensive thing to change; erofs waits for a measurement that asks for it |
| Store front | Bazaar as a Flatpak; web apps as `.desktop` entries in app mode | Nothing to package; GNOME Software and Discover are too heavy |
| How a feature is defined | `features/NAME.toml` (packages, services, modules, `switchable`, `addon`, `health`, `why`); image definitions (format 2) list features and the ones shipped `off`; an add-on is a feature with `addon = true`; code that talks to a feature sits in `features/NAME.rs` in Settings and shell-ui | Adding or dropping is one file, its modules and one line; one format for baked and added features (ADR-008) |
| Files written by another release | Checkers are strict; writers refuse only what they are asked to write; boot apply, the compositor, shell-ui and the updater keep unknown keys, use defaults for unknown values and names, and report them; writes use `toml_edit`; `release.toml` ignores unknown fields; a format bump leaves `settings.toml.v<N>` and, for the manifest, a stepping-stone release | The old slot is the reader that matters, and it cannot be patched afterwards |
| Defaults | An absent key means the release decides; one order (user file, machine file, profiles, preset, release default); writers write only choices; `edel settings reset` and Reset; `docs/defaults.md` generated, its diff in every PR summary and reviewed before each release | Better defaults reach every machine that did not override them |
| Setting names | Named after what the user sees (`services.ssh = false`, `layout.tiling`), never the implementation; never removed or renamed within a format | A swapped implementation keeps every user's value |
| Merged `/usr` | From M7.1, images are built with Alpine's `merge-usr` unless Alpine has merged by then | Developer mode and add-ons need every OS file under one `/usr` overlay; one overlay beats four (Simple) |
| When both slots fail | GRUB starts one anyway with `edel.safe`, which skips the developer layer and add-ons until reset (M7.1) | They live on `/data`, shared by both slots; a safe start that deletes nothing keeps principle 1 |
| ssh host keys | `ed25519` only (`features/ssh/etc/conf.d/sshd`) | Instant: an RSA host key takes a random, sometimes long time to make on the first boot, longest on old hardware, while ed25519 takes milliseconds; every ssh client since 2014 reads ed25519 |
| The way in | One question list and one disk planner in `edel::settings` behind Settings, the terminal and the console; settings files from a path or an `https://` URL only; an installed desktop starts with ssh off | Every door asks the same things and shows what it will erase |
| Enterprise | The same system at home and at work, appliances first; the ten-year channel and a systemd adapter add-on wait until someone can carry them (ADR-009, M11) | Alimardon's choice (2026-10-06); Simple and Efficient keep consumers' images as they are |
| Parts | Every part can be changed, dropped or stand on its own; parts meet through files and standard protocols only, checked in CI (ADR-010, M8.13) | Alimardon's wish (2026-10-06); Simple (no plugin API, one repository until a part has an outside user) |
| Size classes and touch sizes (M5.6c) | Compact below 600 logical px wide, Medium 600 to 839, Expanded from 840; targets 24 px with a pointer, 44 px in touch mode; touch mode as `touch-mode` under `io.github.alimardon123.edel` in the settings portal | Android's window size classes and the touch sizes Apple's and Google's guides agree on, so developers learn nothing new (Simple, Functional) |
| Translations | gettext catalogues, one per part, read by a small Rust reader of the `.po` files themselves, chosen by `region.language` (M5.24) | Alimardon's choice (2026-10-06): the format translators know, one format for every part (Simple). Reading `.po` rather than compiled `.mo` was Claude's default in M5.24a: one file format and no build step, at a few kilobytes more per language; undo by compiling with `msgfmt` in the image build and reading `.mo` in `edel::i18n` |
| Data encryption | On by default for laptop and desktop installs, with a passphrase typed at install; servers and VMs plain unless asked (M8.2) | Alimardon's choice (2026-10-06): the same security for everyone (ADR-009); unattended machines cannot type a passphrase |
| Release lists | Expire 60 days after signing, renewed by every publish and every monthly rebuild; the machine's channel is checked, `updates.channel` or else the image's own; `https://` or a file only (M3.8) | Reliable: a stale or crossed list is refused instead of trusted for ever |
| Time | busybox `ntpd` at boot and daily (M1.13) | No new package (Simple, Efficient); an old laptop's clock must be right for `https://` updates |
| Slots after install | Byte-identical to the signed image, found by partition (M1.12) | Reliable: keeps a verified root possible (ADR-009) before anyone installs |
| Names | The Settings app's names on the command line and in the file: `edel settings`, `settings.toml` linked from `/etc/edel/`, `[layout]`, `[displays.NAME]`, `[region]`, `[default_apps]` and the rest of ADR-008's same names decision, its exceptions listed there (M5.25) | Alimardon's choice (2026-10-06): "If possible, same, if not possible, similar"; Simple (one name for each thing) |
| Tab completion | bash with `edel`'s completion on desktop and laptop images, kept if a slot grows by 3 MiB or less (M5.26) | Alimardon's choice (2026-10-06); Efficient sets the limit |
| Live session | The desktop image logs a person called `live` in by itself when it starts from a removable disk or when nobody has an account yet; `live` is a system account kept on the data partition, never in the slot or the settings file; installed machines, whose people have accounts, show the greeter (M3.6) | Functional: a desktop nobody can log in to shows nothing, and VirtualBox's disk is not removable; Reliable: an installed machine is unchanged |
| Test laptop | Alimardon's HP 250 G8 (Intel i7-1165G7, Iris Xe graphics, 16 GB, from about 2020), started from a USB stick only; the speed reference stays an about-2016 laptop with integrated graphics, still to be chosen (buying one waits for Alimardon) | Real hardware now (Reliable, Functional), while Instant still needs the older reference |

## Risks and how we retire them

- **smithay API churn and musl builds.** Package and crate names are from memory; the first compositor PRs may spend days on dependencies. Pin a version, split the skeleton in two PRs, keep the winit backend for development, say "check in CI" everywhere, correct the notes afterwards.
- **llvmpipe is not a GPU.** CI proves correctness and catches regressions; frame times, VRR and scanout on real hardware come only from Alimardon's laptops through `edel report`. Keep the CI frame budget conservative.
- **The bootloader is outside the rollback.** A GRUB binary that does not start leaves no slot reachable. Every transition runs in CI against every kept release's ESP (M3.5); loader changes are rare and reviewed; `BOOTX64.EFI.prev` is the manual way out.
- **Nothing has run on real hardware yet.** Every PR so far was proven in a VM under software rendering, and the outside review of 2026-10-06 found that apps could not use the GPU, which CI could not see. M3.6 puts the desktop on a USB stick before publishing, M5.19 gives CI a check for the GPU path, and the reference laptop's `edel report` gates each milestone once Alimardon names it.
- **OpenRC early boot ordering.** Services that write `/var` before `edel-data` mounts, or read `/etc` before the overlay, fail quietly. Three separate PRs (M1 steps 2 to 4) isolate each failure; our own init script via `mkinitfs -i` is the fallback with a written reason.
- **Alpine package gaps** (greetd, pam-rundir, gtk4-layer-shell, portal-wlr, Bazaar, fcitx5, squeekboard, FEX). A missing package turns a one-PR step into a packaging step that creates `packages/` and `ci/packages.sh`.
- **No logind.** Some portals or NetworkManager paths may insist on it; then elogind and perhaps polkit enter with the paragraph principle 3 demands.
- **CI time.** Desktop image, Flatpak downloads, the upgrade matrix over kept releases and six test suites can pass runner limits. Caches (cargo, runtimes), split jobs, nightly-only suites for arm64, fleet and old runtimes.
- **Flathub reachability and runtime size.** Cache runtimes or build a local test repo on the runner; the default app set is pulled once at build time.
- **Signing key held by one person.** A lost key strands every machine; two public keys in every image from M1 and an offline backup before the first public release.
- **The `/etc` overlay keeps a machine's old copy of a file whose default changed.** Our own defaults move to `/usr`; `edel settings export` shows the diff.
- **Phones.** Duranium boots SDM845 phones from a GPT inside `userdata` through U-Boot's UEFI; whether GRUB's try counter works the same way is unproven (M9.7 tests it first), and devices without U-Boot's UEFI would need a second counter. Nothing here is provable without hardware.
- **State on `/data` is shared by both slots.** The `/etc` upper layer, `settings.toml`, developer mode and add-on choices survive a fallback, so the slot fallback cannot undo them; lenient boot readers (ADR-008), Reset and `unset`, and the safe start (M7.1) are the answer.
- **Accessibility weight.** Orca and its dependencies may break the Efficient budget; a documented exception until a lighter reader exists.
- **Scope.** Ten milestones and about 89 PRs is a year or more of unattended work; the plan is adjusted in place rather than followed blindly, and the log below says what changed.

## What is deliberately not in the plan

RAUC, ostree or systemd (a systemd adapter only as an add-on, M11.6); a shell extension API, or a scripting layer for the shell as Quickshell's (not now, Alimardon said on 2026-10-06, who may ask for one later; settings, presets and tokens are how the desktop is made one's own, and developer mode is the way to full control); a theme engine or third-party theme format (appearance is a table of tokens); an ISO or squashfs live system; cloud-init, tiny-cloud or cloud-config parsing; polkit, elogind or dconf unless a reused service forces them; delta updates; a package lock or a mirror of Alpine; apk at update time; a Secure Boot shim or Microsoft signing; a long-support channel before M11.5 (ADR-009 records ten years as its goal); an enterprise edition, an enterprise-only security tier, a fleet agent or SELinux (ADR-009); Waydroid and NVIDIA's proprietary driver (NVK is the path); a notification daemon, a dock process, a separate greeter, a session manager script or a settings daemon; a store, a portal backend process or a container tool of our own; a second renderer; a Nix-style store; a Mesa rebuild for newer CPUs or an erofs slot format until a measurement asks for it; a build farm, a mirror network or a website before M8; golden-image screenshot tests; a feature framework (dependencies, versions, hooks or alternatives between features), runtime-loaded code or a plugin API of any kind, feature flags in shipped images, a schema-generated Settings UI, per-domain verbs that change settings (settings change only through `edel settings set` and `reset`), telemetry, a stick writer of our own for Windows or macOS, a look-change notice before the first visible redesign after the first public release (ADR-008).

## Changes to the plan

Dated entries, newest first. Format: `YYYY-MM-DD: what changed, why, which PR`. Since 2026-10-07 only a change to the plan gets an entry: a finished step is recorded by its tick and its "Done with:" note, so the many PRs running side by side do not all add a line at the same place.

- 2026-10-06: two Claude sessions may work on the repository at once, from two of Alimardon's accounts, when one reaches its limits; they cannot see each other, so GitHub is their only link. CLAUDE.md's "Two sessions at once" says how: an open PR, draft or not, claims a step and is opened at the step's first commit; a session coming back from a pause fetches and pushes first and opens no second PR for a step already taken; nobody pushes to the other session's PR. Its "Current state" now runs to #100. An audit of all 100 PRs found nothing lost: every PR is merged except the test-only #30 and #31, the diagnostic #63, and #99, whose commit reached `main` inside #95. Asked by Alimardon. PR #101.
- 2026-10-04: shell-ui's memory budget now counts only what shell-ui holds of its own (`shell_ui_own_mib`: RssAnon plus RssShmem), 1.8 MiB, the 1.5 MiB measured plus 20 percent, in place of `shell_ui_rss_mib`'s 11.2 MiB of VmRSS. VmRSS also counts the pages it maps from its binary, libraries and fonts (8.8 to 10.7 MiB), which the kernel maps more or fewer of from run to run: it read 9.8 to 12.2 MiB on the same code and turned `main` red after #91 and #92 and #95 red, none of which touched shell-ui. The test still reports VmRSS and the file pages; Alimardon's option A of #62 stands as the rule for setting it (measured plus 20 percent). PR #96.
- 2026-10-04: The ten-window frame check (`desktop-test animations`, M5.11b) now budgets what the animations add, `animation_p99_ms`, 1.5 ms (the largest of 23 CI runs, 1.2 ms, plus 20 percent), in place of `frame_p99_ms` for those frames: with motion off alone the runs took 1.7 to 3.8 ms, so the absolute budget measured the runner, and 4.5 and 4.8 ms turned `main` after #91 and PR #95 red without a compositor change, while the animations added 0.5 to 1.2 ms in every run. `frame_p99_ms` still gates the compositor case's frames. PR #99, whose commit reached `main` inside #95, so GitHub shows #99 as closed.

## Principles check

Reliable drives the whole order: the update path, data survival, the watchdog guard, rollback by hand, signing and the fresh-install test come before a single pixel, old releases are kept from the first preview so the compatibility promise has something to be tested against, and every later milestone keeps the A/B, boot, install and upgrade tests green. Instant gets its measurement in the first compositor PR (frame telemetry against a sway baseline, idle frames, a budget) and its mechanism in M5 (effect tiers), not at the end. Simple is kept by the fewest-parts accounting at every milestone, by one system file for the machine and its shell, one kernel, one supervisor per level, add-ons and developer mode sharing one overlay, apk staying in CI, and by taking the apk repo, the lock gate, the migration registry and arm64 out of phase 1. Efficient has budgets before the desktop image exists. Beautiful and Functional follow in M5 and M6 through one token set, a default app set and a basics table with CI checks per audience. Powerful, Scalable and Versatile come after the first desktop, in M7 to M9, as files, add-ons and presets. Traded off: Alimardon waits about 19 PRs for something to look at (they can pull M4 forward), the loader stays outside the rollback and is protected by tests instead, and monthly full-image updates cost bandwidth until a measurement justifies deltas.
